If the yield is not, to a very large extent, employed for the elimination of the inequality in the provision of collective services, with particular emphasis on education, housing, health services and, something I also want to include, agricultural development, the potential for social reform will be lost. Particularly in regard to making land available, not only for housing, but also for agricultural development, the State has a role to play, and the Government will have to accept this role. The abolition of, or at least major intervention in regard to, the Group Areas Act will be essential in order to achieve this. The mere investment of the money obtained from privatisation solely in facets of small business development will not have the desired effect in bringing about stability.</p>

<p>Further constitutional reform is also necessary, but from this point of view it can only contribute towards the removal of discrimination. When we speak of achieving a new dispensation, reform is not the vehicle by which to achieve this&#x2014;we must reach the point of negotiation. Reform, which is one-sided action, no matter what consultation takes place, cannot bring about a new dispensation in an acceptable manner. We are now faced with the dilemma&#x2014;I find this a shortcoming, in the hon the State President&#x2019;s opening address too&#x2014;that there has been no scrutiny of the initiatives that have to be taken for a security strategy and for specific political and constitutional strategies. In an emergency situation it is very difficult to reach the point of negotiation, particularly if security administration is divorced from political and constitutional goals. I am saying, and I have said this in the past too, that the whole search for stability within the framework of a security administration is not possible when it has to function in a vacuum in which there are no clearly identified political goals, even if the goals merely aim at finding a method by which to arrive at certain answers. By the way, in this regard we less strongly endorse the amendment of the hon the leader of the PFP, which blames the Government for not yet having found a solution for Black political rights. We do not think in terms of the solution approach. It seems as if those hon members want to engage in proselytising politics when they say that if everyone merely thought as they did, all the answers would be there. We do not believe that that is possible. Negotiation is an essential requirement.</p>

<p>&#x2020;What we need even more than an economic deal, is a new security management deal. We need, in a sense, to privatise security. We are saying that the individual responsibility and individual opportunity in organising again is very necessary. Political organisation should again be made possible. The responsibility for the collective services&#x2014;to use the parallel again of the developments on the economic policy side&#x2014; should remain with the State and the Government. That means they have to create the space within which this negotiation may take place, and secondly, to get rid of discrimination. Only through negotiation can we really arrive at a new and inclusive deal and a new, all-inclusive constitution.</p>

<p>We believe it is imperative that the Government now take a new look at its security management policy. While we subscribe to the use of special powers&#x2014;I have dealt with this in this House in the past&#x2014;we do not believe that the Government should be entitled effectively to detain people indefinitely. Presently there are a large number of people being detained for activities which are no threat to the State security as such&#x2014;people, political leaders, community leaders, leaders in education who are being detained for no other crime than their being strong and effective political leaders. The Cabinet knows this. There are people presently being detained who will never be charged and who will not be brought to trial. The hon the Minister of Law and Order and the hon the Deputy Minister know this too. I have mentioned names to them at previous meetings, but these people are still in detention. They will not be charged. I challenge the hon the Minister and the hon the Deputy Minister to say anything to the contrary.</p>

<p>Unfortunately I have little time. I would have liked to pursue this matter further.</p>

<p>What we indeed need, however, is a democratic order. The present approach will lead us further along the road towards a more authoritarian rule. Again I want to stress that neither the security police nor the security family are responsible, but only the Government which fails to give directives as to exactly how they should pursue their security goals.</p>

<p>*With the proper implementation of the economic goals, as outlined by the hon the State President, and by properly refining these, we can <span class="col_123-124" refersTo="page_0084"/>get away from what is a self-evident process of economic stagnation.</p>

<p>At present we are in a political <i>cul de sac.</i> We now need a similar process to that which preceded the advent of the economic policy statements by the Economic Advisory Council, by the inclusion of a wide spectrum of opinion in the search for an economic policy. That is what we now need in regard to constitutional development and specifically the security administration campaign. I want to advocate that the Government consider taking the same steps, because I do, in fact, think that this could bring us to a situation of process politics which could jointly lead all of us to the point of a new democratic and inclusive order based on the weighing up of political interests, on the playing of politics. It must be accepted, however, that everyone is included in this.</p>

<p>Neither the moral high ground, nor the occupying of a specific position of power, can bring us to that point. The to and fro arguments about an internal or external system, about relevancy or legitimacy, is not at issue. We know that for the Government sitting there extra-parliamentary politics is totally irrelevant. All its energy, or the major portion of that energy, is employed in dealing with those activities. And conversely, the whole existence of those extraparliamentary groupings, and what they experience as a threat, is the result of the Government&#x2019;s position, which specifically depends on the parliamentary system. The arguments therefore do not hold water. The same applies to legitimacy. Legitimacy is a question of interests&#x2014;what one regards as legitimate relates to where one&#x2019;s interests lie.</p>

<p>I want to conclude by appealing to the Government to also try to achieve in politics, and specifically in regard to the security administration, what it has achieved in the economic sphere in regard to a positive positioning for the future. What we are in greater need of is actually a change of approach, a change of heart. All of us sitting here know that matters cannot continue as they are. The NP has, at least intellectually, taken leave of apartheid. It is true that there are still emotional remnants, which have remained in the form of verligtheid. It is still, however, White control. I now want to advocate that we consider taking leave of verligtheid.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#marais">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P G MARAIS</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon member for Randburg said the following in the course of his speech: &#x201C;We are at present in a political <i>cul-de-sac.&#x201D;</i> Seen from his point of view, that is of course true. Although he is in a political <i>cul-de-sac,</i> we cannot allow ourselves the luxury of his kind of politics. It is for that very reason that we practise the more practical and realistic kind of politics in our country.</p>

<p>The hon member for Randburg attempted, and I believe he did so in all sincerity, to be positive about the hon the State President&#x2019;s opening address. I, in fact, all of us, appreciate that and it is good that it is so. When, therefore, I refer in my speech to the actions and reaction of other opposition parties I do not include him. In the course of my speech, however, I shall be less flattering about him than I am being now.</p>

<p>The hon the State President delivered a fundamental speech at the opening of Parliament. It testifies to the will to govern, it reflects realism, it reflects courage, and it opens up exciting new perspectives. The reaction of the business sector was positive almost throughout. Even opponents of the Government expressed their enthusiasm. By contrast, the reaction of the Official Opposition in this House was unimpressive and unimaginative. It was lacking in new and innovative thought and was merely the same stuffy old approach of years gone by, except that this year it was even worse than in previous years, because the hon the State President&#x2019;s speech caught them completely unawares; that is why they are so inconsolably pathetic. In this instance it would be presumptuous of any opposition party or grouping in this House to move a motion of no confidence in the Government. They should rather consider their own destructive influence on South African public life. Their miserable image of disunity is an indictment against them, in any case. On the right of the NP there are three groups who are actually one group in the spirit. On the left of the NP there are four groups&#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#derby-lewis">

<from>*Comdt <person refersTo="hansard_za">C J DERBY-LEWIS</person>:</from>

<p>How many are there within the NP?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#marais">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P G MARAIS</person>:</from>

<p>Even they do not differ very much from one another. The hon members of the PFP will know that, because they tried to cooperate with everyone. [Interjections.] How is it possible that a group of more or less kindred spirits on the right and on the left of the NP, respectively, can display so much disunity? The answer, at least as far as a section of the participants is concerned, is simple: Intolerance, selfishness, <span class="col_125-126" refersTo="page_0085"/>excessive ambition of leaders and supposed leaders, and the inability of the participants to place their cause above themselves. All these characteristics are unpraiseworthy and objectionable. The existence of it is contaminating the South Africa public life at present. During the past year, especially, we saw a multiplication of it which we could rather have been spared. Owing to a lack of time, I shall confine myself to one particular development in this regard.</p>

<p>The appearance of the so-called Independents dragged an unwelcome element of opportunism and artificiality into our politics. I can cite numerous examples of that, but suffice it for me to cite just one. In March last year the South African public heard that the hon member for Randburg and Dr Dennis Worrall had gotten together. They issued a joint election manifesto in which they said that they were going to rescue the NP and show us how to co-operate. They said they were going to teach us to negotiate and that they were going to save our country for us. The manifesto began with these words:</p>

<block name="quote">There is a new spirit in our country which is yearning for a shared vision for the future.</block>

<p>That gave one the impression that they had this shared vision. Up to and on polling day on 6 May 1987, and afterwards, they held the trusting public under the impression that this was indeed the case. It was not true, however. It became apparent afterwards that these two gentlemen could not stand each other from the very beginning.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#schutte">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">D P A SCHUTTE</person>:</from>

<p>They could not negotiate.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#marais">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P G MARAIS</person>:</from>

<p>They could not live with one another, and they could not negotiate with one another. The hon member for Randburg said, as reported by <i>Die Burger</i> of 12 October 1987:</p>

<block name="quote">In &#x2019;n sekere opsig was hul verbintenis van die begin af &#x2019;n &#x2018;gedwonge huwelik&#x2019;.</block>

<p>[Interjections.] Talk about artificiality, Mr Chairman! Where can one find a better demonstration than that of this concept?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#welgemoed">

<from>*Dr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P J WELGEMOED</person>:</from>

<p>And see how the little one looks! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#marais">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P G MARAIS</person>:</from>

<p>According to the newspaper report the hon member for Randburg went on to say:</p>

<block name="quote">Hoewel hy vroeg reeds aan dr Dennis Worrall ges&#x00EA; het dat hy nie met sekere aspekte van sy program en standpunt saamstem nie, het dr Worrall op sy eerste vergadering op Stellenbosch aangekondig dat hy, mnr Malan, en dr Esther Lategan, &#x2019;n gesamentlike manifes gaan uitgee.</block>

<p>Hon members should note, he did not agree with that, but neither did he dissociate himself from it. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_national_education">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF NATIONAL EDUCATION</person>:</from>

<p>That is shotgun vision! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#marais">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P G MARAIS</person>:</from>

<p>It suited him for the public to think that unity existed, because how would the voters ever be attracted to them if they had a divided vision? Was it genuine, however? No, it was objectionable opportunism at its worst. The hon member for Randburg was also reported as having said:</p>

<block name="quote">My politiek was nog nooit een van opportunisme nie.</block>

<p>What a joke!</p>

<p>When one pages through newspaper cuttings of the past year, one is surprised anew. It unveils a succession of inconsistencies, calculated vagueness, reckless handling of voters&#x2019; confidence, and excessive opportunism. When one criticised them, however, one was accused of being arrogant, and unfeeling and wanting in style.</p>

<p>It was said then that one went for the man instead of the ball. I could have gone on about this for a long time, but I feel that it was an unfortunate low point in our public life which one should rather forget.</p>

<p>I want to talk about the disunity and the confusion in the ranks of the opposition. I repeat, their disunity is an indictment against them. That disunity is perhaps a greater threat to future stability and peace in this country than any of the direct onslaughts with which we are confronted. It creates too wide a spectrum of expectations of what the end result of a process of reform in this country could possibly be. [Interjections.] Whether we like it or not, our options are not unlimited, and they certainly cannot accommodate the diversity of the opposition. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Let us have a look at what the CP is now saying. I am not going to argue with them, because the hon members from that party who are sitting in <span class="col_127-128" refersTo="page_0086"/>this House, are inconvincible. When it comes to them, one can only despair. [Interjections.] It remains a fact that absolute separation in this country between Black, White, Coloured and Asian is not possible. They are managing, however, to get great numbers of people to pursue this pipe dream. A day will still come when they will be accused of having sidetracked people while the future passed them by. [Interjections.] People will find that when they could have done something about their future they were being confused by those hon members to the point where they just went and sat still. A tragedy is unfolding here.</p>

<p>To the hon members of the PFP I should like to say this.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#jacobs">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S C JACOBS</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Speaker, may I put a question to the hon member?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#marais">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P G MARAIS</person>:</from>

<p>No, Mr Speaker.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#van_der_merwe">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J H VAN DER MERWE</person>:</from>

<p>It is an easy one.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#marais">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P G MARAIS</person>:</from>

<p>Last year the PFP tried to win 52 seats in the general election; and they hoped&#x2014;that is what they publicised&#x2014;that when that happened at least 30 of us on this side of the House would walk over to them. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#van_der_merwe">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J H VAN DER MERWE</person>:</from>

<p>You cannot even answer an easy question. [Interjections.] Muil.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_national_education">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF NATIONAL EDUCATION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Speaker, the hon member for Overvaal referred to the hon the Chief Whip as a mule <i>(muil).</i></p>

</speech>

<speech by="#acting_speaker">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">ACTING SPEAKER</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Did the hon member for Overvaal refer to the hon the Chief Whip in that way?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#van_der_merwe">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J H VAN DER MERWE</person>:</from>

<p>I called him by his nickname.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#acting_speaker">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">ACTING SPEAKER</person>:</from>

<p>That is not the point. Did the hon member refer to him in that way?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#van_der_merwe">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J H VAN DER MERWE</person>:</from>

<p>If one has to withdraw nicknames, I shall withdraw that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#acting_speaker">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">ACTING SPEAKER</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member is now trifling with the Chair, and I am not going to allow that. The hon member will first of all apologise and, in the second place, withdraw that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#van_der_merwe">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J H VAN DER MERWE</person>:</from>

<p>What must I apologise for, Mr Speaker?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#acting_speaker">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">ACTING SPEAKER</person>:</from>

<p>Did the hon member call the hon the Chief Whip a mule?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#van_der_merwe">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J H VAN DER MERWE</person>:</from>

<p>Sir, I did not call him a mule. As far as I know &#x201C;Muil&#x201D; is his nickname. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#acting_speaker">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">ACTING SPEAKER</person>:</from>

<p>No, I have given my ruling. Is the hon member going to withdraw that?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#van_der_merwe">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J H VAN DER MERWE</person>:</from>

<p>I withdraw that, Mr Speaker.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#acting_speaker">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">ACTING SPEAKER</person>:</from>

<p>Is the hon member going to apologise?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#van_der_merwe">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J H VAN DER MERWE</person>:</from>

<p>No, Sir, now hold on a minute&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#acting_speaker">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">ACTING SPEAKER</person>:</from>

<p>No, I am not holding on at all.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#van_der_merwe">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J H VAN DER MERWE</person>:</from>

<p>I apologise, Mr Speaker. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#acting_speaker">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">ACTING SPEAKER</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member for Stellenbosch may proceed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#marais">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P G MARAIS</person>:</from>

<p>Before I was interrupted, Mr Speaker, I was trying to point out that the hon members of the PFP believed, before the election last year, that they would be placed in a position in which they could govern the country. I do not want to humiliate them, because they have suffered enough, but I am sure they will concede that all their expectations were wrong. They were dreaming and their dreams did not come true. We warned them that that would happen and they did not listen.</p>

<p>Now I should like to ask them please just to heed this warning of mine. They are dreaming about a so-called &#x201C;non-racial democratic society&#x201D; and a government that is related to this dream is not going to be realised either, and the longer they cling to it the less relevant they will be in White politics in South Africa and the more confusion they will create for the rest of the population.</p>

<p>The same applies to the Independent Movement, which is not here and the National Democratic Movement. The fact is, South African society, with its physical composition and emotional content, limits our options. All too often the Government is accused of lacking the ability to bring <span class="col_129-130" refersTo="page_0087"/>about reform in accordance with one particular model or the other. It is not the Government who should shoulder the blame for that, however. South African society in itself has an inherent inability to be pushed too far in opposition to its own nature, and whoever tries to do that will cause a build-up of tension to breaking point.</p>

<p>There is a lesson in this for the left-wing and the right-wing groups. Their &#x201C;pushing process&#x201D; of South African society will lead unavoidably to breaking point and, notwithstanding the fact that it is not their goal, will lead to an autocracy in South Africa and the destruction of democracy.</p>

<p>It is only the NP which has chosen a realistic path and which stands a chance of bringing about peace in this country. Our path is not an easy one. It is not a highway, but, for sure, it has a destination.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#derby-lewis">

<from>*Comdt <person refersTo="hansard_za">C J DERBY-LEWIS</person>:</from>

<p>It is a dead end street.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#marais">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P G MARAIS</person>:</from>

<p>Finally, that brings me back to the disunity in the ranks of the opposition. On the side of the right wingers unrealistic expectations are being created amongst especially White South Africans of Afrikaans origin. On the side of the left wingers the same is being done amongst Black South Africans and foreign powers. That is hampering the only realistic possibility of reform. It is damaging our country&#x2019;s best interests&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#malcomess">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">D J N MALCOMESS</person>:</from>

<p>Are you suggesting that we should become a one-party state?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#marais">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P G MARAIS</person>:</from>

<p>&#x2026; internally and externally. That is why I say it is absurd to conduct a debate here on no confidence in the Government. It is the opposition parties&#x2014;all of them&#x2014; who really deserve no confidence. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Speaker, it is customary for the Official Opposition to move a motion of no-confidence in the Government. The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition, therefore, made his speech in accordance with the custom that has developed under our system. I do not think anyone can deny him the right to do that, and no one can blame him for actually having done so. Still, I think it is arrogant and presumptuous just to let a motion of no-confidence suffice. The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition and his party have another responsibility as well, possibly one which is greater, viz to discuss the alternatives.</p>

<p>In typical fashion the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition made a speech which failed to address the key aspects of the problems facing the country. Apart from that, he said more by what he concealed than by what he actually said. I should like to quote just one statement made at a Press conference which the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition held on 21 January this year. I am quoting from the report and I should like to couple what I want to say to what I am about to quote:</p>

<block name="quote">In the event of the CP coming into power it would negotiate from a position in which the Whites would enjoy political power in their own areas.</block>

<p>He goes further, Sir, and this is important:</p>

<block name="quote">Should attempts be made to veto the necessary changes in the Constitution to implement CP policy, it would take the necessary steps.</block>

<p>This statement by the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition, read together with the speech he made today, is more frightening in its scope and meaning for the development of the country, than any speech I have yet heard. In the first place the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition is very clearly referring in this regard to the entrenched provisions of the Constitution, because it is only with regard to the amendment of the entrenched provisions that a majority vote in all three Houses is required. Now we must remember the that hon the Leader of the Official Opposition and his party do not want to negotiate about the inclusion of other communities in the country in parliamentary and executive authority and the accompanying processes. The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition and his party want to negotiate with people who are at the moment functioning within the system on their exclusion from participation in that system.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#van_der_merwe">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J H VAN DER MERWE</person>:</from>

<p>Of course.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member for Overvaal has just confirmed the point I made. Moreover, they want to negotiate with the leaders of the Black communities&#x2014;and this is a pertinent issue&#x2014;on their permanent exclusion from that system.</p>

</speech>

<adjournment>

<p>In accordance with Standing Order No 19, the House adjourned at <recordedTime time="1988-02-08T18:30:00">18h30</recordedTime>.</p>

</adjournment>

</debateSection>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#proceedings_of_the_house_of_representatives">

<heading><span class="col_131-132" refersTo="page_0088"/><i>PROCEEDINGS OF THE HOUSE OF REPRESENTATIVES</i></heading>

<prayers>

<narrative>Prayers&#x2014;<recordedTime time="1988-02-08T14:15:00">14h15.</recordedTime></narrative>

</prayers>

<debateSection name="#unavoidable_absence_of_mr_speaker">

<heading>UNAVOIDABLE ABSENCE OF MR SPEAKER</heading>

<summary>(Announcement)</summary>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I have to announce that Mr Speaker is unavoidably absent and that in terms of section 58 (4) <i>(a)</i> of the Republic of South Africa Constitution Act, 1983, he has designated the Chairman of the House of Assembly as Acting Speaker with effect from today to perform Mr Speaker&#x2019;s functions during his absence.</p>

</speech>

<debateSection name="#reports_of_standing_committees">

<heading>REPORTS OF STANDING COMMITTEES</heading>

<p>The ACTING SPEAKER laid upon the Table the Seventh Report, 1987, of the Standing Committee on Trade and Industry, dated 25 November 1987, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Trade and Industry, having considered a draft proclamation relating to the removal of restrictions on economic activities of certain persons in specific circumstances in an area at Kew, Johannesburg, district of Johannesburg, as published in <i>Government Gazette</i> No 10993 of 16 October 1987 in terms of section 1(5) of the Temporary Removal of Restrictions on Economic Activities Act, 1986, and referred to it on 16 November 1987 in terms of Rule 22A, begs to report that it recommends that the proclamation be issued.</block>

<p>The ACTING SPEAKER laid upon the Table the Fifth Report, 1987, of the Standing Committee on Provincial Affairs: Natal, dated 12 January 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Provincial Affairs: Natal, having considered draft proclamations seeking to amend the Local Authorities Ordinance, 1974 (Ordinance 25 of 1974), the Water Services Ordinance, 1963 (Ordinance 27 of 1963), the Durban Extended Powers Consolidated Ordinance, 1976 (Ordinance 18 of 1976), the Nature Conservation Ordinance, 1974 (Ordinance 15 of 1974), the Commissions Ordinance, 1966 (Ordinance 26 of 1966), and the Horse Racing and Betting Control Consolidation Ordinance, 1957 (Ordinance 28 of 1957), and draft proclamations on the total or partial repeal of the Weenen Differential Rating Powers Ordinance, 1961 (Ordinance 19 of 1961), of certain redundant private ordinances providing for extended powers and loan authorities, and of all redundant appropriation, part appropriation, additional appropriation and unauthorised expenditure ordinances promulgated since 1910, referred to it on 18 November 1987 in terms of Rule 22A, begs to report that it has approved the proclamations.<br/>With regard to the draft proclamation relating to the Local Authorities Ordinance, 1974, the Committee, mindful of the aspirations of people of colour, recommends that every effort be made by all concerned, including the Natal Executive Committee, to effect equitable and acceptable participation of all races at third-tier level in the shortest possible time.</block>

</debateSection>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#reports_of_standing_select_committees">

<heading>REPORTS OF STANDING SELECT COMMITTEES</heading>

<p>Mr J DOUW, as Chairman, presented the Seventh Report, 1987, of the Standing Select Committee on Finance, dated 2 December 1987, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Finance having considered the subject of the <i>Financial Institutions Second Amendment Bill</i> [B 114B&#x2014;87 (GA)], referred back to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill with an amendment [B 114C&#x2014;87 (GA)].<br/>Your Committee further wishes to report that it is of the opinion that clauses 3 and 6 of the Bill should be further investigated with a view to further amendments, and that there should be reported back to the Committee in this regard.</block>

<p><i>Financial Institutions Second Amendment Bill</i> [B 114C and D&#x2014;87 (GA)]&#x2014;[B 32&#x2014;88 (GA)] to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr R O&#x2019;REILLY, as Chairman, presented the Seventh Report, 1987, of the Standing Select Committee on Justice, dated 1 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Justice having considered the subject of the <i>Criminal Law Amendment Bill</i> [B 119&#x2014;87 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill with amendments [B 119A&#x2014;87 (GA)].</block>

<p><span class="col_133-134" refersTo="page_0089"/><i>Criminal Law Amendment Bill</i> [B 119A and B&#x2014; 87 (GA)]&#x2014;[B 31&#x2014;88 (GA)] to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr I RICHARDS, as Chairman, presented the First Report of the Standing Select Committee on Constitutional Development, dated 13 January 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Constitutional Development having considered the subject of the <i>Municipal Accountants&#x2019; Bill</i> [B 3&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill with amendments [B 3A&#x2014;88 (GA)].</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr G N MORKEL, as Chairman, presented the First Report of the Standing Select Committee on Environment Affairs, dated 1 December 1987, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Environment Affairs having considered the subject of the <i>Town and Regional Planners Amendment Bill</i> [B 4&#x2014; 88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill without amendment.</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr G N MORKEL, as Chairman, presented the Second Report of the Standing Select Committee on Environment Affairs, dated 1 December 1987, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Environment Affairs having considered the subject of the <i>Forest Amendment Bill</i> [B 6&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill without amendment.</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr G N MORKEL, as Chairman, presented the Third Report of the Standing Select Committee on Environment Affairs, dated 2 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Environment Affairs having considered the subject of the <i>State Land Disposal Amendment Bill</i> [B 7&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill without amendment.</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr G N MORKEL, as Chairman, presented the Fourth Report of the Standing Select Committee on Environment Affairs, dated 2 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Environment Affairs having considered the subject of the <i>Sea Fishery Bill</i> [B 10&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill with amendments [B 10A&#x2014;88 (GA)].</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr R O&#x2019;REILLY, as Chairman, presented the First Report of the Standing Select Committee on Justice, dated 1 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Justice having considered the subject of the <i>Enforcement of Foreign Civil Judgments Bill</i> [B 5&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill without amendment.<br/>Your Committee recommends that the possibility of dealing with interim judgments in a similar way be investigated.</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr A E POOLE, as Chairman, presented the First Report of the Standing Select Committee on Manpower and Mineral and Energy Affairs, dated 22 January 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Manpower and Mineral and Energy Affairs having considered the subject of the <i>Unemployment Insurance Amendment Bill</i> [B 8&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill without amendment.</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr C A WYNGAARD, as Chairman, presented the First Report of the Standing Select Committee on Transport and Communications, dated 1 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Transport and Communications having considered the subject of the <i>South African Transport Services Amendment Bill</i> [B 11&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill without amendment.</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr M R E LEWIS, as Chairman, presented the First Report of the Standing Select Committee on Education, dated 2 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Education having considered the subject of the <i>Boxing and Wrestling <span class="col_135-136" refersTo="page_0090"/>Control Amendment Bill</i> [B 15&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill without amendment.<br/>The Standing Committee has taken note of representations made, not germane to the Bill under consideration, concerning the application of the principal Act to boxing in South Africa. In particular problems appear to be experienced in the relationship between the Control Board and individual boxers and promoters, etc.<br/>In the light of these representations the Standing Committee recommends that the Department of National Education fully consider these representations and that any further amendments that may be necessary be submitted to Parliament.</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr A WILLIAMS, as Chairman, presented the First Report of the Standing Select Committee on Security Services, dated 3 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Security Services having considered the subject of the <i>Police Amendment Bill</i> [B 16&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill with amendments [B 16A&#x2014;88 (GA)].</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr J DOUW, on behalf of the Chairman, presented the Fourth Report, 1987, of the Standing Select Committee on Foreign Affairs and Development Aid, dated 2 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Foreign Affairs and Development Aid having considered certain memoranda referred to it, your Committee begs to recommend that the House approve the following:</block>

<ol>

<li>(1) The description of the area mentioned in the schedule, in terms of section 2 (4) of the Development Trust and Land Act, 1936 (Act No 18 of 1936), as amended, as an area in which the State President may declare released areas for the purposes of the said Act.</li>

</ol>

<p>SCHEDULE</p>

<p>DESCRIPTION OF A CERTAIN AREA OF LAND SITUATED IN THE DISTRICT OF INANDA, PROVINCE OF NATAL</p>

<p>The portion of the farm Groeneberg 844, situated within the following boundary description:</p>

<block name="quote">From Trig Beacon No 38 on the common boundary of the farms Groeneberg 844, Inanda 818 and Inanda Mission Reserve 4579 in a north-eastern direction along the common boundary of the farms Inanda 818 and Groeneberg 844 to the point where it intersects the Emavoti Stream; thence north-eastwards along the middle of the said Emavoti Stream to the point where it intersects the north-western boundary of Subdivision 390 of the farm Groeneberg 844; thence northwards along a straight line to the common beacon of the farms Groeneberg 844, Inanda 818 and Buffels Draai 829; thence in a northeastern direction along the southern boundary of the farm Buffels Draai 829 to the intersection with the western road reserve boundary of the Verulam/ Brookdale road; thence south-eastwards along the said road reserve boundary to the intersection with the eastern boundary of the farm Groeneberg 844; thence southwards along the said boundary to the south-eastern beacon of Subdivision 250 of Groeneberg 844; thence westwards along the boundaries of the following Subdivisions of the farm Groeneberg 844 so as to include them in the area: Subdivisions 250, 265, 309, 308, 310, 311, 312, 319, 323, 325, 320, 274, 270, 269, 268, 267, 266, 264, 263, 260, 326 and 221 to the south-western beacon of the said Subdivision 221, the point of beginning.</block>

<ol>

<li>(2) The description of the area mentioned in the schedule, in terms of section 2 (4) of the Development Trust and Land Act, 1936 (Act No 18 of 1936), as amended, as an area in which the State President may declare released areas for the purposes of the said Act.</li>

</ol>

<p>SCHEDULE</p>

<p>DESCRIPTION OF A CERTAIN AREA OF LAND SITUATED IN THE DISTRICTS OF WARMBATHS AND CULLINAN, PROVINCE OF TRANSVAAL</p>

<p>Beginning at the north-western beacon of the farm Rietfontein 214 JR; thence <span class="col_137-138" refersTo="page_0091"/>north-westwards, northwards and generally north-westwards along the boundaries of the following farms so as to include them in this area: Enkeldoornspoort 207 JR, Zaagkuilfontein 204 JR, Rust der Winter 180 JR, Rust der Winter 178 JR and Tambootie Pan 175 JR, to the northwestern beacon of the said farm Tambootie Pan 175 JR; thence north-eastwards, south-eastwards and generally eastwards along the boundaries of the following farms so as to include them in this area: The said Tambootie Pan 175 JR, La Rochelle 177 JR, the said Rust der Winter 180 JR, Rooikop 181 JR, Leeuwkraal 184 JR and Bezuidenhoutskraal 166 JR, to the north-western beacon of Portion 2 of the said Bezuidenhoutskraal 166 JR (Diagram SG A 959/26); thence southwards along the boundary of the said Portion 2 of Bezuidenhoutskraal 166 JR to the southern beacon (indicatory) thereof; thence southwards along a straight line to the south-eastern beacon of Portion 6 of the said Bezuidenhoutskraal 166 JR (Diagram SG A 3101/25); thence westwards, southwards, south-eastwards, north-eastwards and generally south-westwards along the boundaries of the following farms so as to exclude them from this area: Christiaansrus 191 JR, Ougoed 186 JR, Rietvallei 185 JR and Boschkloof 203 JR, to the southernmost beacon of the farm Melkhoutfontein 183 JR; thence southwestwards along a straight line to the southernmost beacon of the farm Zaagkuilfontein 204 JR; thence further south-westwards along a straight line to the north-western beacon of the farm Rietfontein 214 JR, the point of beginning.</p>

<p>Your Committee begs to report further that the Government is requested to pay attention to the need for housing within and on the boundaries of KwaNdebele for people of colour employed by the industrial sector.</p>

<p>Report to be considered in Committee of the Whole House.</p>

<p>Mr I RICHARDS, on behalf of the Chairman, presented the First Report of the Standing Select Committee on Foreign Affairs and Development Aid, dated 1 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Foreign Affairs and Development Aid having considered the subject of the <i>Borders of Particular States Extension Amendment Bill</i> [B 9&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill with amendments [B 9A&#x2014;88 (GA)].</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr I RICHARDS, on behalf of the Chairman, presented the Second Report of the Standing Select Committee on Foreign Affairs and Development Aid, dated 1 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Foreign Affairs and Development Aid having considered the subject of the <i>Foreign States Immunities Amendment Bill</i> [B 12&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill without amendment.</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#reports_of_select_committees">

<heading>REPORTS OF SELECT COMMITTEES</heading>

<p>Mr R O&#x2019;REILLY, as Chairman, presented the Report, 1987, of the Select Committee on the <i>Marriage and Matrimonial Property Law Amendment Bill</i> [B 120&#x2014;87 (GA)], dated 19 October 1987, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Joint Committee on the <i>Marriage and Matrimonial Property Law Amendment Bill</i> having considered the subject of the <i>Marriage and Matrimonial Property Law Amendment Bill</i> [B 120&#x2014;87 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill without amendment.<br/>Your Committee wishes to report further that it requests the Minister of Justice to consider extending the provisions of section 7(3) of the Divorce Act, No 70 of 1979, to marriages automatically entered into out of community of property, with special reference to marriages entered into outside the Republic.</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr F E PETERS, on behalf of the Chairman, presented the Report, 1987, of the Select Committee on the Electoral Act, 1979, dated 19 January 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Joint Committee on the Electoral Act, 1979, having considered the subject referred to it, your Committee begs to report as follows:<br/>At its last meeting various proposed amendments <span class="col_139-140" refersTo="page_0092"/>to the Electoral Act, 1979, were still under consideration, and the Committee was of the opinion that further oral evidence was necessary.</block>

<p>In the circumstances, and because an extensive investigation being undertaken by the Department of Home Affairs at the request of the Committee is still in progress, the Committee has found that it will not be able to complete its enquiry before the prorogation of Parliament, and your Committee accordingly requests the House to appoint a select committee at an early stage in the next session to form part of a joint committee to resume and complete the enquiry.</p>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#motion_of_no_confidence_in_the_ministers_council">

<heading>MOTION OF NO CONFIDENCE IN THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</heading>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I move:</p>

<block name="quote">That the House has no confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council because&#x2014;</block>

<ol>

<li>(1) it has failed to fulfil the mandate from the electorate in dismantling apartheid and negotiating with the government of the day in establishing a new political, economic and social order for all South Africans; and</li>

<li>(2) of its insistence in linking the repeal of the Group Areas Act with the proposed postponement of the elections to 1992, thus demonstrating its inability to negotiate.</li>

</ol>

<p>In view of the fact that the air conditioning is out of order, however, I should like to suggest that the debate be now adjourned.</p>

<p>Agreed to.</p>

</speech>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#adjournment_of_house">

<heading>ADJOURNMENT OF HOUSE</heading>

<summary>(Motion)</summary>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_house">

<from>*The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, although I was looking forward to this debate, which of course is going to be a very heated debate, I move:</p>

<block name="quote">That the House do now adjourn until tomorrow, 9 February.</block>

<p>Agreed to.</p>

</speech>

<adjournment>

<p>The House adjourned at <recordedTime time="1988-02-08T14:21:00">14h21.</recordedTime></p>

</adjournment>

</debateSection>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#proceedings_of_the_house_of_delegates">

<heading><span class="col_141-142" refersTo="page_0093"/><i>PROCEEDINGS OF THE HOUSE OF DELEGATES</i></heading>

<prayers>

<narrative>Prayers&#x2014;<recordedTime time="1988-02-08T14:15:00">14h15.</recordedTime></narrative>

</prayers>

<debateSection name="#unavoidable_absence_of_mr_speaker">

<heading>UNAVOIDABLE ABSENCE OF MR SPEAKER</heading>

<summary>(Announcement)</summary>

<p>The CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE announced that Mr Speaker was unavoidably absent and that in terms of section 58 (4) <i>(a)</i> of the Republic of South Africa Constitution Act, 1983, he had designated the Chairman of the House of Assembly as Acting Speaker with effect from today to perform Mr Speaker&#x2019;s functions during his absence.</p>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#reports_of_standing_committees">

<heading>REPORTS OF STANDING COMMITTEES</heading>

<p>The ACTING SPEAKER laid upon the Table the Seventh Report, 1987, of the Standing Committee on Trade and Industry, dated 25 November 1987, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Trade and Industry, having considered a draft proclamation relating to the removal of restrictions on economic activities of certain persons in specific circumstances in an area at Kew, Johannesburg, district of Johannesburg, as published in <i>Government Gazette</i> No 10993 of 16 October 1987 in terms of section 1(5) of the Temporary Removal of Restrictions on Economic Activities Act, 1986, and referred to it on 16 November 1987 in terms of Rule 22A, begs to report that it recommends that the proclamation be issued.</block>

<p>The ACTING SPEAKER laid upon the Table the Fifth Report, 1987, of the Standing Committee on Provincial Affairs: Natal, dated 12 January 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Provincial Affairs: Natal, having considered draft proclamations seeking to amend the Local Authorities Ordinance, 1974 (Ordinance 25 of 1974), the Water Services Ordinance, 1963 (Ordinance 27 of 1963), the Durban Extended Powers Consolidated Ordinance, 1976 (Ordinance 18 of 1976), the Nature Conservation Ordinance, 1974 (Ordinance 15 of 1974), the Commissions Ordinance, 1966 (Ordinance 26 of 1966), and the Horse Racing and Betting Control Consolidation Ordinance, 1957 (Ordinance 28 of 1957), and draft proclamations on the total or partial repeal of the Weenen Differential Rating Powers Ordinance, 1961 (Ordinance 19 of 1961), of certain redundant private ordinances providing for extended powers and loan authorities, and of all redundant appropriation, part appropriation, additional appropriation and unauthorised expenditure ordinances promulgated since 1910, referred to it on 18 November 1987 in terms of Rule 22A, begs to report that it has approved the proclamations.<br/>With regard to the draft proclamation relating to the Local Authorities Ordinance, 1974, the Committee, mindful of the aspirations of people of colour, recommends that every effort be made by all concerned, including the Natal Executive Committee, to effect equitable and acceptable participation of all races at third-tier level in the shortest possible time.</block>

</debateSection>

<p>Mr E ABRAMJEE, as Chairman, presented the Seventh Report, 1987, of the Standing Select Committee on Finance, dated 2 December 1987, as follows:</p>

<p><i>Financial Institutions Second Amendment Bill</i> [B 114C and D&#x2014;87 (GA)]&#x2014;[B 32&#x2014;88 (GA)] to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr P C NADASEN, on behalf of the Chairman, presented the Seventh Report, 1987, of the Standing Select Committee on Justice, dated 1 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<p><span class="col_143-144" refersTo="page_0094"/><i>Criminal Law Amendment Bill</i> [B 119A and B&#x2014; 87 (GA)]&#x2014;[B 31&#x2014;88 (GA)] to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr R S NOWBATH, as Chairman, presented the Report, 1987, of the Select Committee on the <i>Marriage and Matrimonial Property Law Amendment Bill</i> [B 120&#x2014;87 (GA)], dated 19 October 1987, as follows:</p>

<p>The MINISTER OF THE BUDGET, as Chairman, presented the Second Report, 1987, of the Standing Select Committee on Constitutional Affairs, dated 21 January 1988, as follows:</p>

<p>Mr P C NADASEN, as Chairman, presented the First Report of the Standing Select Committee on Education, dated 2 February 1988, as follows:</p>

In particular problems appear to be experienced in the relationship between the Control Board and individual boxers and promoters, etc.<br/>In the light of these representations the Standing Committee recommends that the Department of National Education fully consider these representations and that any further amendments that may be necessary be submitted to Parliament.</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr M THAVER, as Chairman, presented the First Report of the Standing Select Committee on Security Services, dated 3 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<p><span class="col_147-148" refersTo="page_0096"/>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<p>Mr Y I SEEDAT, as Chairman, presented the Fourth Report, 1987, of the Standing Select Committee on Foreign Affairs and Development Aid, dated 2 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Foreign Affairs and Development Aid having considered certain memoranda referred to it, your Committee begs to recommend that the House approve the following:</block>

<ol>

<li>(1) The description of the area mentioned in the schedule, in terms of section 2 (4) of the Development Trust and Land Act, 1936 (Act No 18 of 1936), as amended, as an area in which the State President may declare released areas for the purposes of the said Act.</li>

</ol>

<p>SCHEDULE</p>

<p>DESCRIPTION OF A CERTAIN AREA OF LAND SITUATED IN THE DISTRICT OF INANDA, PROVINCE OF NATAL</p>

<p>The portion of the farm Groeneberg 844, situated within the following boundary description:</p>

<block name="quote">From Trig Beacon No 38 on the common boundary of the farms Groeneberg 844, Inanda 818 and Inanda Mission Reserve 4579 in a north-eastern direction along the common boundary of the farms Inanda 818 and Groeneberg 844 to the point where it intersects the Emavoti Stream; thence north-eastwards along the middle of the said Emavoti Stream to the point where it intersects the north-western boundary of Subdivision 390 of the farm Groeneberg 844; thence northwards along a straight line to the common beacon of the farms Groeneberg 844, Inanda 818 and Buffels Draai 829; thence in a northeastern direction along the southern boundary of the farm Buffels Draai 829 to the intersection with the western road reserve boundary of the Verulam-/Brookdale road; thence south-eastwards along the said road reserve boundary to the intersection with the eastern boundary of the farm Groeneberg 844; thence southwards along the said boundary to the south-eastern beacon of Subdivision 250 of Groeneberg 844; thence westwards along the boundaries of the following Subdivisions of the farm Groeneberg 844 so as to include them in the area: Subdivisions 250, 265, 309, 308, 310, 311, 312, 319, 323, 325, 320, 274, 270, 269, 268, 267, 266, 264, 263, 260, 326 and 221 to the south-western beacon of the said Subdivision 221, the point of beginning.</block>

<ol>

</ol>

<p>SCHEDULE</p>

<p>DESCRIPTION OF A CERTAIN AREA OF LAND SITUATED IN THE DISTRICTS OF WARMBATHS AND CULLINAN, PROVINCE OF TRANSVAAL</p>

<block name="quote">Beginning at the north-western beacon of the farm Rietfontein 214 JR; thence north-westwards, northwards and generally north-westwards along the boundaries of the following farms so as to include them in this area: Enkeldoornspoort 207 JR, Zaagkuilfontein 204 JR, Rust der Winter 180 JR, Rust der Winter 178 JR and Tambootie Pan 175 JR, to the northwestern beacon of the said farm Tambootie Pan 175 JR; thence north-eastwards, south-eastwards and generally eastwards along the boundaries of the following farms so as to include them in this area: The said Tambootie Pan 175 JR, La Rochelle 177 JR, the said Rust der Winter 180 JR, Rooikop 181 JR, Leeuwkraal 184 JR and Bezuidenhoutskraal 166 JR, to the north-western beacon of Portion 2 of the said Bezuidenhoutskraal 166 JR (Diagram SG A 959/26); thence southwards along the boundary of the said Portion 2 of Bezuidenhoutskraal 166 JR to the southern beacon (indicatory) thereof; thence southwards along a straight line to the south-eastern beacon of Portion 6 of the said Bezuidenhoutskraal 166 JR (Diagram SG A 3101/25); thence westwards, southwards, south-eastwards, north-eastwards and generally south-westwards along the boundaries of the following farms so as to exclude them from this area:<span class="col_149-150" refersTo="page_0097"/>Christiaansrus 191 JR, Ougoed 186 JR, Rietvallei 185 JR and Boschkloof 203 JR, to the southernmost beacon of the farm Melkhoutfontein 183 JR; thence southwestwards along a straight line to the southernmost beacon of the farm Zaagkuilfontein 204 JR; thence further south-westwards along a straight line to the north-western beacon of the farm Rietfontein 214 JR, the point of beginning.</block>

<p>Your Committee begs to report further that the Government is requested to pay attention to the need for housing within and on the boundaries of KwaNdebele for people of colour employed by the industrial sector.</p>

<p>Report to be considered in Committee of the Whole House.</p>

<p>Mr Y I SEEDAT, as Chairman, presented the First Report of the Standing Select Committee on Foreign Affairs and Development Aid, dated 1 February 1988, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Foreign Affairs and Development Aid having considered the subject of the <i>Borders of Particular States Extension Amendment Bill</i> [B 9&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill with amendments [B 9A&#x2014;88 (GA)].</block>

<p>Bill to be read a second time.</p>

<block name="quote">The Standing Committee on Foreign Affairs and Development Aid having considered the subject of the <i>Foreign States Immunities Amendment Bill</i> [B 12&#x2014;88 (GA)], referred to it, your Committee begs to report the Bill without amendment.</block>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#conduct_of_members">

<heading>CONDUCT OF MEMBERS</heading>

<summary>(Statement)</summary>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Before I call upon the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition I want to address hon members on a matter that has been a cause for deep concern to me for some time. In my experience no-confidence debates can become rather heated and sometimes harsh words are spoken. In my opinion it is a good thing that hon members have this opportunity to let off steam and throw a few political punches and I have no intention of stopping the fight even before it has started.</p>

<p>However, I do want hon members always to bear in mind that freedom of speech in debate and proceedings is one of the most cherished and significant privileges of Parliament. The privilege allows members great freedom in debating whatever matter is before the House at any particular time, and in their speeches to expose and criticise whatever they think should be exposed or criticised. This freedom cannot be questioned in any court or place outside Parliament.</p>

<p>However, Parliament&#x2019;s own rules, customs and practices require of hon members not to abuse this right but to use this freedom in such a manner that the dignity and honour of Parliament will be upheld and protected at all times.</p>

<p>Every member of Parliament should therefore under all circumstances temper his speech and his utterances in debate so as to conform to the Parliamentary standards of propriety. Hon members have a duty not to make imputations, insinuations and even accusations of improper conduct on the part of their fellow members or to implicate their fellow members without the matters complained of being clearly stated or the complaints being properly substantiated, if at all. If it is considered at all necessary to make imputations and accusations of improper conduct, this must be done in accordance with the rules, customs and practices of Parliament which, in appropriate cases, require a substantive motion. In doing so, they will uphold not only their own dignity but also the status of Parliament. I appeal to all hon members kindly for their co-operation.</p>

</speech>

</debateSection>

<debateSection name="#debate_on_motion_of_no_confidence_in_the_ministers_council">

<heading>DEBATE ON MOTION OF NO CONFIDENCE IN THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</heading>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I move the motion printed in my name on the Order Paper, as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">That the House has no confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council of the Administration: House of Delegates and especially its Chairman.</block>

<p>I want to refer briefly to the notice of motion proposed by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, which refers specifically to one item.</p>

<p><span class="col_151-152" refersTo="page_0098"/>Unfortunately, Mr Chairman, we who are in public office are accountable.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>It does not refer to one item. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>What I am saying is that when we enter public office we are under public scrutiny and we are accountable to the public. [Interjections.] I have never pointed a finger at anyone in this House. However, I feel I have a duty as a representative of the electorate to say in this House what my constituents expect me to bring to the attention of hon members seated here.</p>

<p>Moreover, we cannot lose sight of the fact that a tremendous amount of publicity has been given in recent weeks and months to all kinds of accusations. I am not saying that they are either founded or unfounded, but the whole matter has been left dangling in midair and that reflects poorly on all of us whether we are on this side of the House or the other. [Interjections.] I think it is in the interests of all concerned that these matters should be cleared up once and for all.</p>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has recently faced a barrage of accusations that would, in any normal situation, have resulted in a commission of inquiry to either prove or disprove once and for all the very serious allegations, not only about the administration of the House of Delegates but also about the involvement of a member of the general Cabinet in the Government of this country in associating his role with such far-reaching accusations. It is not good arguing that the House should pass a motion calling for such a commission. No accused person in any society, even if he is innocent, is given the right to decide whether a trial proving or disproving his innocence should take place. The accusations have acquired disturbing dimensions. Unresolved, they reflect not only on the entire Indian community but also on the integrity of the South African Cabinet. If there is any false accusation, let a commission of honourable and respected judges clear the air once and for all. [Interjections.] Suspicion and rumour are worse than guilt. The innocent need not fear the establishment of their innocence. It is only the guilty that would place obstacles in the path of the purification process. [Interjections.] Allegations of corruption and maladministration ultimately reflect on the body as a whole. Hence the Cabinet itself is unnecessarily stigmatised by the failure to clear the air. It is now the hon the State President&#x2019;s clear duty to disprove the detractors of a member of his Cabinet. I say respectfully that failure to do so would amount to a vote of no confidence in all of us who share a common ideal with him, namely that of a clean and honest administration. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, I make that statement with all sincerity, because I would like to see an end to this on-going fighting. It will go on amongst public representatives as long as there are things which remain unclear. That is my appeal.</p>

<p>I understand that on Friday afternoon when we left the Chamber certain allegations were levelled against me. We left because the hon the Leader of the House called for the adjournment of business and it was unopposed. Business of the House was adjourned at that point. We demonstrated our protest at the fact that proceedings were allowed to continue. Accusations which were levelled &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council had sought and received permission to congratulate a newly elected member as soon as the Leader of the House stood up and proposed the adjournment of the House. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! That is not a point of order.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I once again want to reiterate what I said in 1984 and repeated in 1985. If there is anything which the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council or any other hon member of this House holds against me, I give this undertaking to the House: I open myself to an inquiry, which can be submitted to the Attorney-General or the Auditor-General. [Interjections.] What is more, I submit to any inquiry, including the reference of those papers to the Advocate-General, and I do not want to be represented; I do not want to utter a word. The papers will exonerate me and prove that what has been done is all above board. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, all I am saying is this: It is unfair to bring to the floor of the House matters which were dealt with in 1985. If members of the ruling party feel that they have something against me, I am prepared to submit myself to an open inquiry. I have said that over and over again. [Interjections.] <span class="col_153-154" refersTo="page_0099"/>What is more, I will walk out of this House and resign if something can be proved against me. [Interjections.] I ask the hon member pointing a finger to accept the challenge and do likewise. I repeat what I said in 1985.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Remember that you are using the word &#x201C;proof&#x2019;.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Proof, yes.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You are qualifying it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>No, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council can do whatever he wants to do. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the newspapers have been full of stories.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Well, this photograph does not come from us. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I appeal to hon members to give the hon member an opportunity to continue addressing the House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, in support of my motion, which is directed against the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and his colleagues, I want to say that many things have happened which reflect very poorly on this Administration. Only those who are oblivious to the truth will continue to ignore these matters. When reports appear in the Press, and, as in the <i>Sunday Tribune,</i> affidavits are published, signed by people who are not afraid to reveal who they are, affidavits which are a shocking condemnation&#x2026;[Interjections]&#x2026; a reflection on all of us &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>No, Mr Chairman. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>This revelation is a condemnation of all of us who are seated here and who are participating in this House. This is the point I want to make. [Interjections.] No hon member in the House of Delegates is exonerated&#x2014;we will all be tarred with the same brush for matters of this kind. In the newspaper report it says that a senior official of the Department of the Administration is involved. I cannot see how reasonable men can take umbrage here where we are presented with the opportunity of bringing it to the notice of the party in power across the floor of this House. I cannot understand why people become angry about these things as there is nothing to become angry about. What is stated there is the truth.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>We are not angry about that. If it is wrong, it is wrong.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I want to point out again that we all have to carry the stigma on our shoulders. We must all accept responsibility because in the final analysis it refers to the House of Delegates and we are all hon members of this House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>We support you in not condoning wrongs. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I want to come back to Tongaat. I will be failing in my duty if I did not re-enact the drama that took place in Tongaat during the elections. I cannot see how on earth a lecturer from a teachers&#x2019; training college, a headmaster and a whole cavalry of teachers who were supposed to be on duty could be actively engaged on polling day at the polling-booth, receiving voters etc. [Interjections.] I believe this must be examined and an end put to it once and for all because I do not think we can allow such a situation to continue. It is merely the tip of the iceberg. [Interjections.] The question that I want to pose is: What right do these teachers have to leave their schools and the children at a time when the examinations are near and the pupils should be receiving the greatest possible attention and preparation for the exams? Yet one finds the teachers outside, actively working for a political party with all sorts of paraphernalia on their persons.</p>

<p>I guarantee to every member of the teaching fraternity his right to support some political party and his right to political views &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; <span class="col_155-156" refersTo="page_0100"/>COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>But it is only right to support solidarity!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>No, no. [Interjections.] I have said on every occasion that I spoke that we as a political party guarantee to the teacher his right to vote and to identify himself with the political party of his choice. I will only be too pleased and happy to know that a certain teacher supports a party and I guarantee him this right. I guarantee every teacher the right to participate and to vote for whoever he pleases. However, what I am against is when a teacher is used as a means to an end. That is to promote a particular party.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Take your mind back to 1984!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>We do not want to go back to 1984. The Tongaat saga happened only two months ago and presents a far clearer picture. [Interjections.] What I am saying is that we cannot afford such a situation. Whether one likes it or not, it looks like and can be called patronage. It places a tremendous onus on the vast majority of the teaching fraternity as to whether they want to be supporters of political parties in order to progress in their particular professions or, on the other hand, if they stayed aloof and carried out their work in accordance with the regulations prescribed, they would be penalised in one form or another.</p>

<p>It is a very serious situation and what I am saying is that education must be removed from the drama of politics. I have said, and my chairman has also warned, that nobody can say that the situation with regard to the party will remain as it is forever. There is bound to be an election shortly. However, if we as members of Solidarity take office&#x2014;and this is a commitment we are making&#x2014;we will legislate if necessary in order to redress those wrongs that have been visited on people who depend on the integrity and the impartiality of the administration. That commitment we make from here and we want people to know that that is the way we feel about the matter. At the same time we guarantee to every teacher the right to vote freely for whomsoever he pleases. That is another guarantee.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>That has nothing to do with this matter. Please keep it out of this. Show some respect.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You are the dirtiest man in this House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I request you to ask the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council to refrain from making comments which might be an indictment of my religious convictions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, if that is his conclusion, it is a conclusion of his mind.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the drama of education is also entering other fields and more recently the hon Minister of National Education reminded people that politics must not be introduced into the school arena.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, I must crave your indulgence. I need to have some water to drink.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I shall see to it. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>That is sour grapes. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I think those remarks from hon members are uncalled for and I ask hon members to refrain from uncalled for remarks such as &#x201C;sour grapes&#x201D;. What was done here shows a relationship between colleagues. The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition may continue.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman I appreciate that, these are the kind of qualities we want to come to the fore increasingly in the future. After all as the hon the State President once said to us, one may argue and differ very strongly but when one leaves this Chamber that must be the end of it, because we are all human beings. I think that is a very worthy piece of advice that all of us can adhere to.</p>

<p>When at the outset I talked about the need for a commission of Inquiry to clear the air once and for all, I was not merely doing so for my sake. Here I say that hon members like Mr S Pachai and Mr S Abram also asked the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council in a debate last year that a commission of inquiry should be instituted because they felt that the accusations levelled against them were unfair and unfounded. It does <span class="col_157-158" refersTo="page_0101"/>not matter that today they sit on the other side. An allegation is an allegation and it does not become any less valid when the members involved cross over to the ruling side. I say this with humility because I believe that what is recorded in Hansard will be held against hon members irrespective of the party to which they belong, or whether they are in Parliament or not. Someone will have to answer for accusations levelled.</p>

<p>In column 81 of the Hansard of 22 May, let me tell you what Mr Pachai said.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>The hon the Deputy Minister of Local Government and Housing.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>The hon the Minister of Local Government and Housing. I cannot see him at the moment in the House. He said, and I quote:</p>

<block name="quote">Thank you, Mr Chairman. I hope you will grant me some injury time, Sir. I was witness to an indignity perpetrated against my colleague, which was one of the last straws that made me decide I was a misfit in the National Peoples Party. When I was tramping the length and breadth of this country, pleading for support for this party, I told people to join because this party was a vehicle for the upliftment of the people of the country. We would attempt to undo the wrongs of the past and see that justice be done. What was the end result of my efforts? I was propping up an individual who was bestowing upon himself powers out of all proportion to the original intent. The hon member for Cavendish touched upon the fact of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council assuming the portfolio of, or acting as Minister of the Budget and Auxiliary Services, while at the same time he was a member of the Cabinet, and at the same time the leader of the party, and at the same time Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. Then&#x2014;this was really the straw that broke the camel&#x2019;s back as far as I was concerned&#x2026;</block>

<p>He goes on to ask questions that need to be answered because they are valid questions. I believe the hon the State President would be doing the community and the country a favour by ensuring that there is an inquiry into the affairs not so much of the whole Ministers&#x2019; Council but of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.
