As far as I am concerned he stands in the dock today accused of bringing about the downfall. I have just referred to this. The Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council responded:</p>

<block name="quote">Who wanted me to interfere with contractors?</block>

<p>That is a very, very serious allegation. That allegation was made against a Deputy Minister. I say this with humility, not to try to score debating points. As long as this statement appears in this edition of Hansard, it is a reflection on that individual. He must be given the opportunity to clear his name on that issue because it has been recorded in Hansard. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I am not trying to gain any benefit from this or to glorify myself as a result of this statement, but the fact remains and it is a serious indictment. I make this submission because I should like the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council to understand why in my opening address I asked for the commission of inquiry I referred to, to be instituted.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Last time he was Mr &#x201C;Pachay&#x201D;, and now he is Mr Pachai. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Would the hon member Mr Thaver withdraw what he has said and apologise.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>I withdraw it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I mention the name of Mr S Abram-Mayet. He has also called for an inquiry because his name is also recorded as being somebody disappointed with the kind of references that were made to him in the course of debates in this House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Take it as read!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>He said if you cannot beat them, join them, so he joined them. I now want to come to the speech that had been made by the late hon member for Tongaat. He asked a series of questions when he spoke here. While putting those questions to the House&#x2014;I want to place this on record&#x2014;he was interrupted by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. Because I want to allege here that those questions that were asked by the hon member for Tongaat&#x2014;and I cleared this with him&#x2014;were put to him on the telephone one evening that week, I did not find it strange that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council had the following to say:</p>

<block name="quote">Has the hon member for Tongaat received <span class="col_159-160" refersTo="page_0102"/>information to the effect that a councillor has indicated that somebody wants to offer a commission of Rl,5 million to anyone who can declare a certain White area Indian?</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>And that is Windmill Park! That was what I was told. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Another commission of inquiry will shut them up! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>We are merely reminding the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and this House, without making allegations against anyone, that these statements are on record. Obviously, when it was stated that must have been the truth, I accept that all statements made here and on record, are statements made by responsible people who are aware of this.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>The police are aware of it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>They are aware of it. Therefore I am not sucking these words out of the air&#x2014;I am merely quoting. I wish to quote the hon member Mr S Abram briefly:</p>

<block name="quote">I challenge the Chairman to agree to an inquiry and I want to tell this House&#x2026;</block>

<p>Thus I also agree that Mr S Abram would be prepared to subject himself to an inquiry&#x2014;that is on record here. Are these not compelling reasons as to why we should have an inquiry? It is not only the hon member for Reservoir Hills. Other people holding responsible positions, holding office in terms of the Constitution, are involved.</p>

<p>I just want to end my reference to the record in Hansard by saying that regarding these stands, we have identified four areas. We are interested in Villa Liza. The advertisement did not indicate the boundary on which we had agreed with the Department of Constitutional Development and Planning. Why is it that somebody is blocking every other area we examine? Who is doing this? This is the concern of the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Just dramatise that, please.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>They are eating Villa Liza like a lollipop!</p>

<p>They are going beserk! Villa Liza! Villa Loser! Mona Lisa! Nothing but Villa Liza!</p>

<p>The chairman of the management committee of Boksburg told me&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You must repeat it as I said it. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Quoting the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council he told me that fantastic commissions are being offered to people just to have it declared Indian. We have decided to buy Villa Liza. The hon member Mr S Abram told me: Do not worry about the southern section of 800 ha; worry about the top. He then told me, in my house, that some Indians had bought land in the southern section. The hon member for Natal Midlands confided in me that the person who took the option to buy those properties was none other than the hon member Mr S Abram. [Interjections.] He even said it in the presence of a witness.</p>

<p>Now, I am merely quoting. Since these allegations have been made against responsible individuals in this House&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Hon members.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>&#x2026; against hon members of this House, I believe these must be cleared up, or else they are part of the record. They will one day be cast at these members in any situation they may find themselves in.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I should like to ask you a question when you are finished.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I wish to refer to Windmill Park. In this House all of us have roundly condemned the Group Areas Act, and the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, when I was a colleague of his in the Ministers&#x2019; Council, decided that whenever land is available for the Indian community, we must buy the land so that we do not experience shortages.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Provided it is affordable and &#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>The intention was clear&#x2014;to try and get as <span class="col_161-162" refersTo="page_0103"/>much land as possible for the Indian community. I have no disagreement with that&#x2014;it is a good thought because people in all socio-economic levels must get land.</p>

<p>However, as far as Windmill Park is concerned my interest is simply that we must not allow the Group Areas Act to prescribe and limit the extent of the land that is made available to us. As many areas as are available should be proclaimed or made available to the Indian community while the Group Areas Act is on the Statute Book.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Not as long as there are middlemen.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I agree with the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. I said at the hearing on Windmill Park that the administration of the House of Delegates must be responsible for the purchase and development of the land.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>An MP of the Assembly whom we had discussions with confirmed that there are middlemen.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>It is our view, that the House of Delegates has the responsibility to provide homes and land for a large section of the population which depends on the State for housing. I made it very clear at Windmill Park that the House of Delegates is the body that will have to acquire the land so that they can plan development for the needs of the Indian community. They have the records. Unfortunately, that was opposed by some colleagues of ours here in Parliament. It was negotiated between certain individuals &#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I want to publicly say that I have opposed it for the time being. [Interjections.] I have letters.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>It was intended to be Coloured area, but &#x2026; [Interjections.] On the other hand, we also have an area near Actonville called Apex where two small areas were identified which could provide a reasonable number of stands for the people of Actonville in close proximity to where they are at present. Here again there were objections, but the strange thing was that a gentleman by the name of Mr Badenhorst who happened to be the proprietor of the larger of the two areas&#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>An HNP supporter whom we&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>He indicated that the hon Mr S Abram had for some time been talking to him with a view to having this land made available for the Indian community. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Not that small section.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Whatever it is, he was surprised that in respect of the land that was now being made available. There was opposition.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, I believe that whatever land is made available to us in the Apex area will give us a stake as adjacent to these two areas are large vacant townships which, I think, have been proclaimed to be serviced and which could very well be brought in. In fact, in my submissions to the Chairman of the Board there, I said that I would like to see those townships that fall within the area of Brakpan, examined as part of a larger group area for Actonville. The town clerk of Benoni indicated his willingness to incorporate these areas should they be proclaimed Indian.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Therefore, for the stand we are taking you should have confidence in us.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Indeed? The town clerk of Benoni indicated that his council was not averse to having this land incorporated into the area of Benoni. This would make it possible for people of Actonville to house themselves in close proximity to the existing areas. I think that was a fine gesture on his part, but what surprises me is that we want to block land coming into the hands of the Indian community. I will make it very clear that as long as there is a need for housing for the lower income group, the responsibility for the purchase and development thereof lies with the House of Delegates. Does the House of Delegates feel that they have enough on their hands and would they rather just wish these private developers good luck?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p><span class="col_163-164" refersTo="page_0104"/>Fair enough. However, other things have subsequently come to my notice which also give rise to great concern. Representations were made to the hon the Deputy Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning concerning the possible removal of the community of Palm Ridge.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Not from the House of Delegates.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>No. However, these representations have been made. [Interjections.] These representations were made by members of Parliament who are members of the House of Delegates. I did not say that the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council or his colleagues made those representations.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Well, then this motion should be amended!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>No, I do not want to amend my motion. As long as anyone involved is associated with the ruling party, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has to carry the can. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Oh, I see, that is your motivation. So I have to carry the can! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>How can anyone&#x2014;and more particularly hon members of this House&#x2014;make representations designed to move a community which has been thrown out of Germiston and resettled&#x2014; not entirely to its satisfaction? They are complaining about a whole lot of things, as I am sure the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is aware. [Interjections.] What I am asking, is how we can so eloquently espouse the cause of our community here&#x2014;the pain and suffering they have experienced as a result of removals under the Group Areas Act&#x2014;and then initiate moves to resettle our own people from Palm Ridge?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>But you concede that no one from the Ministers&#x2019; Council or the administration &#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I made that point, but as I said, they were persons associated with the ruling party and therefore the ruling party must carry the can. Politically, it then unfortunately becomes the responsibility of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>We will take you up on that tomorrow.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I want to say here that if the proclaiming of Villa Liza entails the people of Palm Ridge or anywhere else being led to the sacrificial altar, then it is an act that is so disgusting that no person who claims to be a representative of the Indian community could be a party to such a move. We cannot. I do not want to read those documents, because of the pain that I go through &#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>If you have the documents, could you let me have them on a confidential basis? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Certainly, I will give the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council a copy of the documents. [Interjections.] It pains me that this could happen to our people. We raise hell in this House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Name the member! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I could read the document if hon members would like me to read it out. [Interjections.] It reads as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">Spacial requirements of the Indian community and identification of possible areas.</block>

<p>These are the records of the minutes of a municipality, Mr Chairman. I quote further:</p>

<block name="quote">Attached hereto as Annexure &#x201C;A&#x201D;, is a copy of a letter dated 85. 12. 05 from Mr S Abram-Mayet, MP and Mr A E Lambat, MP, a letter to the Deputy Minister of State Development and Planning. The second last paragraphs of the said letter was treated as confidential at the request of Mr S Abram-Mayet.</block>

<p>[Interjections.] Let me quote from the letter as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">Proposals for consideration, acceptance and implementation.</block>

<ol>

<li>(a) That the Villa Liza area as indicated on <span class="col_165-166" refersTo="page_0105"/>map 1 be accepted as a future home for members of the Indian community.</li>

<li>(b) That occupation and possession be granted by the award of permits in accordance with the group areas law in respect of the portion of the Villa Liza area known as Mapleton Plots and Mapleton Plot Extension 1.</li>

<li>(c) That private town developers be empowered through permit control to proceed to develop the town for members of the Indian group with private capital (as in the case of Rynsoord) with regard to any land inside the Villa Liza area which has not in accordance with the group areas law been declared White.</li>

<li>(d) That a permit will be issued to PBRK Investments (Prop) Ltd to develop the proposed Villa Liza towns for Indian occupation and possession on condition&#x2014;<br/>That the company will sell 700 erfs (dwelling units) to the Department of Local Management, Housing and Agriculture of the Administration of the House of Delegates at production cost plus a reasonable margin for the housing of Indian members in the subeconomic and lower economic groups from the Benoni and Germiston magisterial districts and such other people as the department may deem fit.</li>

<li>(e) That those members of the Indian community residing in Palm Ridge will be provided with replacement housing in the Villa Liza-towns by the Department of Local Management, Housing and Agriculture of the Administration of the House of Delegates.</li>

</ol>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>The letter or the minutes?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>The minutes. The letter is an extract of &#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Have you got a copy of the letter?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I will give it to you.</p>

<p>I quote further:</p>

<ol>

<li>(f) That the Department concerned will provide housing in the Villa Liza-towns for Indians in the sub-economic and lower economic groups at present residing in Germiston, Boksburg, Brakpan and Benoni Municipal areas and experiencing a housing crisis.</li>

<li>(g) That Palm Ridge then be made available, preferably to approved private township developers for the extension of Katlehong, Tokoza, Vosloorus Black Townships if it is considered a matter of national interest.</li>

</ol>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Can you read the letter which your party secretary sent to me, asking me to do a deal with contracting developers?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>It reads further:</p>

<ol>

<li>(h) That all attempts to acquire portions 35 and 36 of the Modderfontein Farm IR for the occupation and possession of the Indian community will cease.</li>

</ol>

<p>Messrs S Abram-Mayet, MP and A E Lambat, MP request the Council to support the above-mentioned proposals.</p>

<p>This is an excerpt of the correspondence.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>That is the minutes. I will give you the actual letter.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I do not know if I have the actual letter.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>But that memorandum you were quoting from is not by the hon member Mr Abram.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>No, it is the contents of a letter. It says here, it is &#x201C;copy of a letter&#x201D;.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>It is a memorandum prepared by a consultant.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>It says here, and I quote:</p>

<block name="quote">Attached hereto as ANNEXURE &#x201C;A&#x201D;, is a copy of a letter dated 85. 12. 05 from Mr S Abram-Mayet, MP and Mr A E Lambat, MP. The letter to the Deputy-Minister of State <span class="col_167-168" refersTo="page_0106"/>Development and Planning is the second last paragraph &#x2026;</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>But they never proposed &#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I will give the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council a copy of this. [Interjections.] But these records are floating around, Mr Chairman. It is a serious indictment. Not only that&#x2026; [Interjections.] This is a serious matter to me and I take it seriously. No hon member of this House should have the temerity to sacrifice the public for his own personal benefit. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>For too long the working class people have been taken for a ride and this has to come to an end. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, with your protection, I now want to read from an article which appeared in <i>The Sunday Star.</i> These things are wrong. Somebody must come forward and explain these things to us. In the absence of any explanation or refutal of these allegations as they stand, they form the basis for an inquiry.</p>

<p>I read from <i>The Sunday Star,</i> 27 August 1986, and I quote:</p>

<block name="quote">MPs Back Removal Plan<br/>Privately sponsored plans to uproot a community and resettle it on land in which at least one Member of Parliament has an interest emerged this week during a <i>Sunday Star</i> investigation into a new Indian township on the East Rand.<br/>Plans to establish the Villa Liza township&#x2014;on and between Boksburg and Brakpan&#x2014;have already involved a police investigation into allegations of bribery against members of the Boksburg Town Council Management Committee.<br/><i>The Sunday Star</i> investigation revealed that attempts were under way to persuade the Government to move the Indian community out of Palm Ridge, Germiston&#x2014;where they have been living for less than 18 months&#x2014;to Villa Liza.<br/>The attempt was sponsored by a company called PBRK Investments and has been recommended and endorsed by two House of Delegates MPs.<br/>Palm Ridge residents have reacted angrily, saying it was a form of &#x201C;forced removals&#x201D;, dreamed up by private enterprise and &#x201C;certain individuals&#x201D; who stood to make millions if the Government approved the scheme.<br/>The plan is now so advanced that it is subject only to final approval by the House of Delegates and the Department of Constitutional Development and Planning. A decision on the matter is pending.</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Shall we now close our case?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Wait, let me continue quoting. I will give credit to the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. I continue quoting:</p>

<block name="quote">As the growing row threatened to engulf Cabinet Minister Mr Amichand Rajbansi, he visited the troubled Palm Ridge township on Tuesday.<br/>He assured residents that nothing had been finalised yet but warned at the same time that Palm Ridge may have to be moved.<br/>Investigations by <i>The Sunday Star</i> this week revealed:</block>

<block name="quote">A town planning report commissioned by PBRK Investments was sent to the Department of Constitutional Development and Planning in Pretoria last year, recommending that Palm Ridge residents be moved so that the township could be used for the Black group area of Katlehong.</block>

<p>The report recommended that a new Indian township&#x2014;Villa Liza&#x2014;be approved on the East Rand between Brakpan and Boksburg. It would provide an alternative for the residents of Palm Ridge as well as provide additional housing for the Indian community of Actonville, Benoni. A further recommendation was that a permit be granted to PBRK Investments to develop the township.</p>

<p>Mr Rajbansi, Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council in the House of Delegates, has admitted the Villa Liza concept is under consideration, but said no decision had been taken to move Palm Ridge residents.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from><span class="col_169-170" refersTo="page_0107"/>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Until the PBRK was removed by me.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I quote further:</p>

<block name="quote">PBRK Investments has two directors listed at the Registrar of Companies in Pretoria, a Mr Phillipus Boshoff Rudolph Kuypers and Mrs Elsabe Kuypers. A link between the company and a House of Delegates MP, Mr Salem Abram-Mayet, emerged during the investigation.<br/>The town planning report&#x2014;researched by a Prof Jan Schutte&#x2014;recommends that the development of Villa Liza be carried out by means of a permit as this would help to avoid the publicity which would &#x201C;normally surround&#x201D; the proclamation of a group area near white towns.<br/>Mr Abram-Mayet obtained details of and approval for the use of land owned by the Brakpan Town Council for the establishment of an Indian township. The land for which Mr Abram-Mayet was negotiating was that which the Schutte Report identified as Villa Liza. The Brakpan Town Council said in its letter to Mr Abram-Mayet that the market price of the land was some R3,5 million.<br/>In March of this year, following allegations that members of the Boksburg Town Council had been bribed to approve a new Indian group area outside the town, a complaint was laid with the commercial branch of the Police at John Vorster Square. The investigation is now complete and the docket is presently with the Attorney-General pending a decision on prosecution. No further details were available this week. Council sources said the complaint was linked to Villa Liza.<br/>The Labour Party MP for Actonville, Mr A E Lambat, has been actively involved in the campaign to secure Government approval of Villa Liza to be developed. He and Mr Abram-Mayet wrote a joint letter to the Benoni Town Council last year recommending the Council&#x2019;s support of the Schutte Report.<br/>Actonville has a chronic housing shortage but a community organisation there, the Actonville Watchdog Committee, says it is opposed to the private development of new Indian group areas, claiming it would cost residents almost twice as much as it would if the State was responsible.<br/>Approached for comment this week, Mr Rajbansi said no area on the East Rand had been finally approved for the development of a new Indian group area. He said that Villa Liza was &#x201C;one of four different areas&#x201D; under consideration.<br/>Despite repeated attempts over three days to reach Mr Abram-Mayet, he was not available for comment.</block>

<p>I am not saying that what is in this report finds Mr Salem Abram-Mayet guilty. That would be the last thing that I would say. What I am saying is that if political figures are drawn in this way, I think we have a duty to give them an opportunity to clear their names. The only way in which we can do that is to hold a commission of inquiry because the matter of Villa Liza&#x2014;I am not making any allegations now&#x2014;has implications as are indicated in these reports and in the latest developments.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Do you know that there is no stink in Villa Liza but there is one in Windmill Park, and your secretary is involved in the latter? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>This is the second time that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council makes this allegation. I have no interest in Windmill Park. The records of the hearings at Benoni and Boksburg on Windmill Park make the position very clear. The hon the Leader of the PRP advised initially, as I also did, because any land that can be obtained for the Indian community is welcome as somebody could make use of it. However, we made it absolutely clear that in so far as I was concerned it was the responsibility of the House of Delegates to be the party responsible for the purchasing of it and the housing of the people.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition take a question? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>No, Sir.</p>

<p>I have brought this to the notice of the House because I have listened to people who have made submissions before these committees and it is clear that they feel a genuine concern for the <span class="col_171-172" refersTo="page_0108"/>welfare of the Indian community, the House of Delegates and in so far as our communities and the problems we have are concerned.</p>

<p>We must not add any more stigmas to this institution, because then a finger is pointed at everybody which is totally unfair. I say no individual has the right to take it upon himself to negotiate for this or that land and in the process also to commit himself to certain developments which hurt our people. That pains me and as the Leader of the Official Opposition it is my duty to bring this to the notice of the House. I am doing so without pointing an accusing finger and I want this to be very clearly understood. I am not accusing anybody, I am merely stating the facts and I believe that it is the duty of a commission to investigate these matters and clear the air for all concerned. Such a development can only redound to the good of this House and all of us who serve in it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I think that it is the height of stupidity for the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition to come along here and submit a motion of no confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council and especially its Chairman after they have received one of the finest thrashings that they could have hoped for in a by-election by losing a seat which they had held in 1984.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Tell the Chairman how you got the seat.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Sir, I want to tell them how we won the seat. We did not win on special votes, but we won that seat outright as a result of the majority of the voters voting for the NPP on the election day. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>We had 1 438 special votes and the total number of votes cast were 3 368. If one subtracts 1 438&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member for Stanger must contain himself.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I would like to draw your attention to the fact that the hon member Mr Abram used a word with reference to the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition which I think is unparliamentary. He used the word &#x2018;stupid&#x2019;, obviously referring to the hon Leader. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, if I have said a word which is unparliamentary I withdraw it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Did the hon member use the word the hon member for Stanger is now referring to, implying that the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition is stupid?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I said that it was the height of stupidity on the part of the Opposition to come along with a motion of this nature. I do not see anything wrong with the statement I am making. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I would like the hon member to withdraw that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Fine Sir, I withdraw it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#viyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J VIYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I put a question to the hon member?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, waste my time.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman does the hon member remember everything he said against the hon Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council on 31 May 1987?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, today is 8 February 1988 and a lot has changed during that period, just as those hon members in 1986 decided to introduce a wishy washy motion before this House in place of the customary motion of no confidence.</p>

<p>Things have changed for them too because there was a question of sour grapes. Many people there were ambitious and did not get where they wanted to get to so they decided to withdraw from the agreement they had made. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>The hon the Leader of the Opposition has made several allegations here today based on information he has gleaned and obtained from irresponsible people on the East Rand. I want to warn the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition of the old adage: One is often judged by the company one keeps. That is the kind of company in which I found the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition last week.</p>

<p>I found it rather strange last week when Mr P J Badenhorst, who is a supporter of the HNP, stood up at a hearing of the Group Areas Board and openly stated that the Indian people wanted separate residential areas. He was loudly applauded by the company the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition was in, but not by the <span class="col_173-174" refersTo="page_0109"/>company in which I found myself that afternoon. We do not say one thing inside the House and then do something else outside. [Interjections.] The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition was elated&#x2014;hon members should have seen him&#x2014;when the hon member of the HNP was speaking&#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>He had no interest other than a passing commercial interest in getting rid of land that was useless to him and to the White community. What is more important is that the land we have been fighting for in that region was owned by this member of the HNP. He made it very clear last week to these hon members that that land was not in question; it was for White development exclusively. It is land that is useless to the Whites that he is keen to palm off onto the Indian community. The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition did not question what the costs would be of development, or whether it is possible to develop the land, whether it is useful or useless. He was led by the nose by those little Solidarity pipsqueaks in that region&#x2014;and there is no solidarity there in any case&#x2014;there are no Solidarity members in the Transvaal. I want to give him the assurance that there will not be any in the Transvaal in future.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, am I to believe from what the hon member Mr Abram has said &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Is there a question? Please ask. Unlike you I am prepared to answer a question.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Badenhorst said you have been sitting in his office asking for that land for a long, long time.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Shame!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Is that true or not? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>The trouble here is that the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition, who always proclaims himself to be a proud South African, does not understand Afrikaans. This is where the difficulty lies. His interpreters who were with him on that day are equally&#x2014;I do not want to use the word which under normal circumstances I would love to have used&#x2014;useless because they do not understand Afrikaans either.</p>

<p>When Mr Badenhorst said I had been running to him for this land I immediately stood up and asked him whether it was not true that he had sent members of the Indian community that he knows to ask me whether I would not be interested in looking at land he was proposing to sell for the Indian community. I went to his farm and I got into his motorcar&#x2014;a silver Toyota Cressida&#x2014; and he took me around to show me the land he had to offer. I made it very clear to him that the land he was offering was not sufficient for the needs of our community. If the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition did not understand that, I think he should go back to school and learn Afrikaans so that he can at least communicate better.</p>

<p>I want to tell the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I be permitted to ask the hon member a very simple question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Is the hon member prepared to take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Any time, Mr Chairman, unlike hon members on that side of the House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>Will the hon member Mr Abram tell this House whether he had a pecuniary interest in Villa Lisa. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member for Phoenix is a very upright member and I have a great deal of respect for him. I wish to be placed on record as saying that I am prepared to subject myself to any inquiry or any type of commission. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>If any commission proves that I have a cent&#x2019;s worth of pecuniary interest in Villa Liza or any other development in that region, I want to give them the assurance that I am prepared to donate whatever I possess to whatever charities that hon member chooses.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member a question? It is a simple one. Will the hon member indicate whether he is prepared to adhere to everything that he has said in this House and that is on record in Hansard?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from><span class="col_175-176" refersTo="page_0110"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon member for Reservoir Hills is supposed to be one of the most intelligent members in this House. After I have spoken, I want to know what more he wants to know from me. I say that if any such independent commission finds that I have had soiled hands, in that I had a pecuniary interest in whatever we have been proposing for the community&#x2014;let me reiterate this for him, because I know that he is deaf in the one ear and he might not have heard me properly&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Are you denying it?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I should like to remind the hon member Mr Abram that he is not allowed to refer to the disability of any hon member.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon member is sometimes hard of hearing. I want to reiterate that I am prepared to donate everything I possess to a charity nominated by the hon member for Phoenix if it is found that I have soiled hands. I do not know what more assurance I should give the hon member for Reservoir Hills. [Interjections.] I should love to see whether the hon member for Reservoir Hills would be prepared to subject himself in the same manner as I have done.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Any time!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>I want to make it very clear that the whole issue of land acquisition in the Transvaal is not an easy one. Whilst we have had all kinds of allegations being bandied about, I want to make it very clear that traditionally, on the East Rand the Government decided that Indian people were to be settled in the jurisdiction of Benoni, and Coloured people were to be settled in the jurisdiction of Boksburg. Because we in Benoni have no land left, we saw fit to negotiate with the authorities of Boksburg. I want to tell the hon the Leader of the Opposition that we had a great deal of difficulty penetrating the Boksburg council to get them to agree that some land in their area of jurisdiction can be made available to the Indian community. It was not easy going.</p>

<p>After we had done all the dirty work, one had all the Johnny-come-latelies, all the little mushroom committees springing up and trying to jump on the bandwagon, on the hard work put in by us. Nobody gives us credit for the hard work that we have put in. The hon member for Camperdown has already been given the answer as far as commissions of enquiry are concerned. I am not afraid of them because I have nothing to hide. I am absolutely clean and I have nothing to hide.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>We welcome that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Whatever I possess I am prepared to subject to the scrutiny of anyone. I hope that hon member is prepared to subject his pigs on his farm to that scrutiny as well. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Can the pigs give evidence?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>I want to remind the hon Opposition that there was a particular councillor in Boksburg who had originally decided upon a plea that we had made concerning Villa Lisa.</p>

<p>Again, Sir, to some of the people these are only concepts. However, to us who live there and who know the region, there is something tangible in these things.</p>

<p>Our problem on the Reef is that much of the land is subject to various conditions. Mining titles are registered over properties etc etc. Much of the land on which beautiful mealies grow&#x2014;as Mr Badenhorst said last week&#x2014;is unfortunately not available for development as townships, because mining rights are registered over this land. We have great difficulties and I have pleaded with everybody against the tendency amongst the authorities to try and placate us by offering us small parcels of land. Hence our problem is resolved for a year or two and then we are back to square one. That is why we suggested that a thousand hectares of land, which is made up of the farms Roodekraal and the ones on which Villa Lisa is supposed to be laid out, would suffice for the reasonable future&#x2014;not that we are in support of the Group Areas Act. These little patches, namely Apex and others, will fall in automatically because there are no White takers for them. However, if hon members wish to go for little blocks and patches now, <i>I give</i> them the assurance that the White man does not love us enough yet to throw everything open. Therefore, we have difficulty in getting the authorities to accept proposals that are made from time to time. I would like to ask the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition not to be led by those fortune-seekers that are actually rallying behind them in the Benoni area&#x2014;the failed people. Failed, because it has been proven beyond all <span class="col_177-178" refersTo="page_0111"/>reasonable doubt that those are the very people who have a profit motive. The whole issue of Villa Lisa and Roodekraal has been through various investigations. It has been considered by the Group Areas Board, amongst others. However, the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition referred to some documentation in which he purports to portray the message that I myself and my colleague, the hon member for Actonville, are somewhere along the line responsible for the possible removal or uprootment of the people of Palm Ridge. Now, there have been ministerial assurances about permanency of Palm Ridge. I cannot, for the life of me, stop any planning consultant from preparing reports and making recommendations as to how he sees the future. I am not a planner of that magnitude to be in a position to make similar contributions. The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition is reading from a document prepared by a consultant. Yet what the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition is not reading to us, is a &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, what I read from &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Does the hon member have a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I would like to know from the hon member concerning this document from which I quoted, where Palm Ridge was referred to, if the hon member is not the author thereof?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Of course I am not the author of that document. I am the author of a letter in which we have made it very clear that whilst we agree with the broad principles espoused in that document, namely the report of the town planning consultant, we are not in a position to make any comments on his references to Palm Ridge. That is the prerogative of the people of Palm Ridge. I think that is fair comment and that the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition will agree with me.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>I agree with what you are saying, but I quoted from a letter &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! That is not a question.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Is the hon Leader of the Official Opposition prepared to let me have a look at that letter?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Is the hon member prepared to let me have a copy of the letter that he is referring to, the one that he and the hon member for Actonville wrote?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>I am quite prepared to let the hon member for Springfield have that copy. It is a very reasonable request. I have nothing to hide.</p>

<p>Now, Mr Chairman, the letter from the Brakpan Town Council which the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition refers to is the one I wish to comment on. Here is a letter dated the 14th of November 1985 which I will pass on to the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition as soon as I have finished quoting from it. Please just let me have it back.</p>

<p>Sir, this local authority owns land.</p>

<p>This local authority owned land in that area and after we had tried to break that local authority down to agree to allow the Indian community some land in that area we tried to tie them down to a figure regarding how much they wanted for their land. We asked them what, in their opinion, the value of their land was. They wrote to us and we submitted this document to the previous Ministry of Housing telling them that this was the land in question and that this was what the local authority was looking for as far as this land was concerned. Unlike the letter which the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition is supposed to have sent to us and which has not yet reached my desk, I am prepared to send this one to him for his perusal because we have nothing to hide. Could someone just take this to the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Could the hon member give me a copy too?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Certainly.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, I want to make it very clear that what has been happening here is that various people, for a variety of reasons best known to themselves, have been doing all sorts of things in an attempt to deter development in that region. However, who is suffering and paying the price at the end of the day? It is the ordinary man in the street, the ordinary wage-earner, who is living under the most ghastly conditions imaginable.</p>

<p>I now want to turn to Windmill Park. I have here in front of me a document which was submitted by some Indian people to the Boksburg Town <span class="col_179-180" refersTo="page_0112"/>Council. I want to know from the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition&#x2014;who consorts with those people&#x2014;whether he agrees with the contents of this document. It has to do with Windmill Park, and this is what they have to say to the Boksburg Town Council:</p>

<block name="quote">Our intention is to create a luxury residential township for dwellings only and as such to exclude sectional title development, flats and commercial development.</block>

<p>[Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>They belong to a luxury party! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Of course! They state further that they would like the conditions of title of this particular township to be amended so that a minimum figure of R40 000 could be included therein. [Interjections.] Those are the people who have been running around with the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition during the past few weeks, and those are the people who told us they would give us such a good thrashing in the Eastern Transvaal that Solidarity would be controlling the Transvaal in the next municipal elections. I want to tell the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition that considering the kind of soldiers he had last week, I feel very sorry for that party. [Interjections.] It has no potential for growth whatsoever.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member whether I did not hear him say at Windmill Park that if that land were declared Indian, I wanted it to be acquired by the House of Delegates for the provision of housing for working-class people?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Honestly speaking, Mr Chairman, the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition did say that, but the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition also said that he could not stand up in Parliament and talk about private enterprise and then not try to implement it elsewhere. Therefore, he is in favour of private enterprise as well.</p>

<p>I now want to come to the sort of private enterprise we are talking about. [Interjections.] I shall tell hon members what this free enterprise entails. This free enterprise works on the following basis. What happens is that a gentleman by the name of &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member whether he is aware of the fact that the authors of that letter have written to me privately, asking me to deal with certain contractors? Those people are associated with Solidarity. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I am very much aware of it. [Interjections.] One of the Solidarity party&#x2019;s top brass in that area, if I may refer to him as such&#x2014;I wonder whether he will still be a member of their top brass after 2 February&#x2014;is connected with a construction firm and he is using the plight of the people to further his own commercial interests.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>He came to see me privately.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Before I move the amendment, I want to tell the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition the following. Those people with whom he is associated have engaged the services of an estate agent, and that estate agent has been going around to the owners of property in that region.</p>

<p>He has been getting them to sign documents. I have copies of agreements which they got people to sign. There is a condition contained in the agreement, namely that it is incumbent upon the purchaser to obtain a permit under the Group Areas Act, 1966, as amended, for occupation and ownership of the property within 120 days from date of signature thereto, subject however to the condition that in the event of the property being proclaimed for occupation and ownership by members of the Indian group, this condition will not apply. What happens, however, is that this so-called Rubicon Mining Company buys up the land and homes for x amount and offers it to our people for y amount. In this particular case, serviced stands which were sold to White people at R20 per square meter, have been offered to and bought by our people at R30 per square meter. This is what that side of the House supports. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition made great play about some permit issue, but he was a member of a so-called ad hoc committee under the chairmanship of the hon member for Boksburg, Mr Blanch&#x00E9;. Again I want to make it quite clear that I accept that perhaps he did not understand what went on, but he attended a <span class="col_181-182" refersTo="page_0113"/>meeting with the hon member for Boksburg in Boksburg, and at that meeting he agreed to Villa Lisa being shifted to the long term, in preference to Windmill Park.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>No, not at all.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Everything that the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition said last week at the Group Areas Board hearing is negated by the fact that he agreed, according to the evidence given by a councillor from the Boksburg Town Council, to the fact that the Villa Lisa issue should be left for the long term. The hon member for Boksburg and the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition have been calling upon the hon the Deputy Minister of Development Planning to support the issue of permits for the 203 plots in Windmill Park, which are being sold as a result of this type of blatant exploitation of a community which is land starved and in need. [Interjections.] The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition must not come now with other side-arguments.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I would like to ask the hon member Abram whether he has any news that the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition went to the Deputy Minister in support of the permit application for those exploitations.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, that is the news we have, namely that the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition called on the Deputy Minister to support these permits. [Interjections.] Before my time is up, I would like to move an amendment to the motion.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, I move:</p>

<block name="quote">To omit all the words after &#x201C;That&#x201D; and to substitute &#x201C;the House recognizes that&#x2014;</block>

<ol>

<li>(1) the Chairman and all the members of the Ministers&#x2019; Council of the House of Delegates work tirelessly to improve the quality of life of the people and that much progress has been made in respect of the provision of services in the various areas under the jurisdiction of the House of Delegates; and</li>

<li>(2) the image of the House of Delegates has been tremendously enhanced by the performance of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, a fact recognized and appreciated also by most of the members of the opposition parties in the House,</li>

</ol>

<p>and therefore has full confidence in the Chairman and all the members of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.&#x201D;.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, I still have a few minutes left, and I want to use this time to broach another subject. As I explained earlier, traditionally Boksburg was to have housed the Coloured community and Benoni was to have housed the Indian community. In order to penetrate Boksburg, it was also necessary to seek the co-operation of our Coloured counterparts in that town. When we originally wanted to approach the Boksburg Town Council for its support, we approached our Coloured counterparts as well, and there was a gentleman&#x2019;s agreement between the hon member for Actonville, the Actonville Management Committee&#x2014;which, since one of its members has absconded and joined Solidarity, has again changed its mind&#x2014;and ourselves, with the MP for Reigerpark and the Reigerpark Management Committee, that they would not stand in our way for Villa Lisa and Roodekraal and we would not stand in their way for Windmill Park.</p>

<p>I want to ask whether, where we have the functioning of the Group Areas Act and where there is the possibility that friction can be created because we are negating a gentleman&#x2019;s agreement, made by leaders on a local level&#x2014;it is a kind of local option exercise&#x2014;that side of the House wants to contribute towards creating friction within these two communities or does it want to help towards a harmonious South Africa where we can all live together in peace and harmony? I want to allege that the hon members of the Opposition have lost the will to represent their people. In their entire election campaign in the Eastern Transvaal they were not able to portray the image where they could be seen as an alternative administration.</p>

<p>I attended one of their meetings and the hon member for Stanger told some of my friends that it was wrong of me to come to their meeting which was a public meeting.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>I said you are welcome to the meeting. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>I want to have it placed on record that the hon the Leader of the Opposition tried to sell his economic policy etcetera and he did not try to attack on a personal level, but all the hon <span class="col_183-184" refersTo="page_0114"/>member for Stanger could do was to talk about certain Bills that had to be referred to the President&#x2019;s Council. He also told the audience deliberate untruths because when I stood up to question him&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>I answered you!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>No, he did everything else but answer my question. He skirted around the question.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: Is it permissible for the hon member Mr S Abram to allege that the hon member for Stanger told some deliberate untruths?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I have not said that the hon member for Stanger spoke an untruth here. I said that the hon member told deliberate untruths at a political party meeting at Kinross, of all places.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: I wish to be advised by you and not by the hon member.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Did the hon member Mr S Abram say that the hon member for Stanger told a lot of untruths at a political meeting?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, he told deliberate untruths.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I will give a ruling on that at the earliest opportunity.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#abram">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">S ABRAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I will abide by your ruling.</p>

<p>I want to remind the Official Opposition that out at Kinross we are very quick to criticise others for so-called &#x201C;voters fraud&#x201D;. At Kinross 19 special votes were cast without any identification documents whatsoever. Those persons were all introduced by Solidarity workers. What was the result? The returning officer rejected those 19 votes. I want to ask the Official Opposition that before they point fingers at others, they should examine themselves to see that everything is in order in their own camp. Only then should they point fingers at others.</p>

<p>I want to conclude by repeating an Afrikaans saying: &#x201C;&#x2019;n Perd wat trek, kan nie skop nie, en &#x2019;n perd wat skop, kan nie trek nie.&#x201D; This means that the horse that pulls the cart, cannot kick, but the horse that does not pull a cart, kicks. [Interjections.] That is what is happening here. This side of the House has the ultimate political responsibility over this administration and it has to pull the cart. [Interjections.] We now have another pedigreed Arab stallion from the Eastern Transvaal to pull this cart even faster.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, it is evident beyond any doubt after listening to the last two speakers that no hon member in this House would be left unconvinced that there is every need for a judicial inquiry. I would support the motion of no confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council and its chairman for no other reason than the Ministers&#x2019; Council and its chairman not supporting the need arising out of absolute wisdom that where there are such contentious matters members should be able to bring documents to this forum and be able to make allegations and counter allegations.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman I would go further. Probably you as Chairman of this House in your wisdom, which God has blessed you with, should also seriously consider writing to the Speaker or to the hon the State President to really institute an commission of inquiry so that there will be peace and sanity in this House.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, I do not want to go into the rigmarole of citing many individual cases of allegations of corruption, but what I will do for the general good of the House and the ruling party in particular is to make reference to certain broader issues where there are serious flaws and where we find ourselves floundering. Unless it is corrected we will be causing considerable damage to a community which has a history of which this country and other parts of the world can be proud.</p>

<p>In 1984 the hon Mr A Rajbansi who is now Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council gave a solemn undertaking to the Indian community that he was going into Parliament to dismantle apartheid. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>In plain terms this implied that he would oppose and fight the NP and its leadership in every possible way and that is our cardinal reason for participating here, is it not? However, what are the facts?</p>

<p>Instead of opposing the NP the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is found to be supporting it <span class="col_185-186" refersTo="page_0115"/>and instead of fighting it he is defending it in every possible way. Today there is not a more loyal disciple than the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council of the commanding guru&#x2014;the spiritual master&#x2014;Mr P W Botha.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Why do you not tell them what you tell me in private?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, there is abundant proof of his support for apartheid elections in a front-page article of the January 1988 issue of <i>Phoenix.</i> This makes hypocrisy of his assurances to the Indian community of coming into Parliament to dismantle apartheid. He is giving all his support to perpetuate apartheid and racial discrimination.</p>

<p>In the same article he claims that the NPP has consistently campaigned for the scrapping of the Group Areas Act. This is not true. I know for a fact that the hon the Chairman told his guru the hon the State President that the Indian community does not want the Group Areas to be scrapped.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Is that a statement of fact?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>On whose authority did he do this?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I do not have the time to take a question. [Interjections.] If he sends it to me in writing I will give him a reply.</p>

<p>He went to groups of Indians, Sir, and used the threat that if they wanted the Group Areas Act to be repealed he would tell the hon the State President to do so and that he would repeal the Group Areas Act for the Indian people. Mr Chairman, do you call this leadership? Do we want the Group Areas Act to be repealed only for the Indian community?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You are the super salesman of the Nats.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>We want the Group Areas Act to be repealed unconditionally for all people.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>That is not what you tell me privately. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>That is why we said especially for the Chairman, because of this kind of&#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>For once in your life speak the truth!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council finds it convenient to be in the Cabinet when his counterpart in the House of Representatives has vacated his post as a result of this very issue.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>What about your Chairman&#x2019;s swim?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>The Ministers&#x2019; Council, under the leadership of this hon Chairman, had made a mess of education. This nobody could deny. I am not making any allegations against any individual. I am not making any allegations against the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, nor against the hon the Minister, nor against the Director, nor against anybody else. However, there is such strong feeling in the field of education that in order to retrieve the situation and to ensure that education continues in the normal way, there is every need for a judicial commission of inquiry.</p>

<p>Why does the hon the Chairman not really feel concerned about the Indian community and about education in particular, which is such an emotional aspect of the Indian community? Why do they not have the courage or the strength, the wisdom or the foresight, not to allow this? As human beings we might have made some blunders, as might the ruling party, the Official Opposition, and some officials, but let us clear this up. We have read of a case in which an inspector helped a pupil to get top marks. I am sorry to mention that this is a typical case. But these are cases that really should be talked about so that the education body will be able to allay some anxiety and pupils would know where they stood.</p>

<p>Previously promotions, transfers and appointments were made on a democratic basis following a democratic system where the whole inspectorate had a say. What is the position today? It has become exactly the opposite. It has become absolutely autocratic, where one person makes unilateral decisions on the direct instruction of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>
