<p><span class="col_187-188" refersTo="page_0116"/>I would like this to be proved and this can be done only if there is a judicial commission of inquiry. Hon members on the other side of the House are not blessed with wisdom. There is a spark of divinity in every one of them. We have our weaknesses but we have the gift of God within every one of us. Let us manifest that to some extent and say let us go ahead.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You are a bitter man because you are not sitting here.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>I am not bitter. [Interjections.] This shows how far the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council will go to defend his particular interest. Not the community&#x2019;s interest, but his own. You will not have a commission of inquiry yet you can accept this system in which a single man manipulates the educational system of promotion, transfers and appointments. He is directed to manipulate for party-political gain, not for educational improvement.</p>

<p>The reason for the clash between the Executive Director and the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is too obvious. [Interjections.] Noone can deny this. Besides, our department makes external appointments. There is no need for this undue delay in the appointment of the Chief Executive. It is not in the interest of the Indian community nor in the interest of Indian education. Promotions, transfers and even appointments of teachers are not done on merit. I need only ask for evidence as to who those people are that have been advanced to positions of acting principalship this year. It will be a revelation. The teachers&#x2019; morale is at an all-time low. Why? Teachers are used as pawns in a game during elections. The conscientious teachers are feeling terribly embarrassed. Those teachers are also supporting the ruling party for petty gains. There are no two ways about it. After all, they are human beings. Promises are given such as: &#x201C;Give me 15 or 50 votes and I will give you this position.&#x201D; They are seen visiting schools in advance.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Name the person.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>There are many people. The hon member for Isipingo. Is the hon member happy now? If you appoint a judicial commission of inquiry I already have two names. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Who is the gentleman? Would you welcome the judicial commission of inquiry as my hon colleague did?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Given us the names and we shall support you. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>What do you mean, &#x201C;cover up&#x201D;? I am not like you, I do not cover up. Several teachers, for instance, are taking full-time leave. Do you want me to force these people to come here and make allegations? I think a stop must be put to these things.</p>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council should not consider his personal security in this regard or his party interests.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Can you look me in the eye when you talk to me privately?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>He should rather consider the common good. There is blunder upon blunder, and here I refer to 1986 and 1987. I refer to the placing of professional teachers in temporary posts. There were 1000 such cases last year. These teachers have been occupying substantive posts; they should be put on permanent staff after a probationary period. Why this indictment of the Indian Education Department?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Blame the Chairman.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>The Chairman is not responsible.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>The overall effect of all this is that there is a decline in the standard of education. I am going to explode the myth put about by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council in even approaching business firms to advertise the success of this House of Delegates in education. I say that if the members of the House of Delegates are doing their job and we have attained a certain level of, shall we say, achievement, then what need is there to go to X, Y and Z and tell them or pay them to advertise?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Are you getting jealous? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Are we not going to ridiculous lengths? We are going to shameful lengths, if I may say so. There are also other departments that now do this.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; <span class="col_189-190" refersTo="page_0117"/>COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Your speech has now gone to that level.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>There is serious indiscipline in schools today. Nobody can deny that. Take random schools in Phoenix. In an article in yesterday&#x2019;s newspaper a principal says he does not want his name to be mentioned but that there are terrible things happening. There are conflicts and there is violence on an unprecedented scale. This is being compounded by provocation and a decline in morale. Despite this the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council sits here and makes remarks without taking serious note of this crucial situation that has now arisen. The Opposition has been generous enough to bring this to their attention. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I hope he has appointed somebody to take real note of these things. Then he can have a study group meeting tomorrow, shift the responsibility and blame this on the community.</p>

<p>However they are not interested in the community. They are satisfied where they are. There is massive unemployment &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#palan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">T PALAN</person>:</from>

<p>Will the hon member take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, my time is running out. I have made it very clear, the hon member may send his question to me in writing.</p>

<p>The Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council year after year has boasted about the standard of matric results. The foundations therefore were laid in the lower standards&#x2014;he must know common psychology. Anybody with some educational background is aware of this fact.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rampersadh">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">H RAMPERSADH</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member be prepared to take my advice &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! There is no such thing as taking advice. Will the hon member please resume his seat. The hon member for Cavendish may continue and will the hon members please remember that he is not prepared to take any questions at this stage.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Thank, you, Sir. I made reference to newspaper adverts where glowing accounts of these results were given. There is no need for that. We would like these results to be authenticated by an inquiry in that very department. I can tell hon members from my contact with personnel members in that department that there is widespread dissatisfaction about the adjustment of these marks. In 1987, more than in any other year, promotions have been approved of on the lower level, with the result that the percentage pass mark has been boosted. More than that, the percentage by which these marks have been adjusted, is unrealistic. I have that figure right here, but I do not wish to mention it. I am sure the executive director and his department would like to carry out an investigation on an issue as sensitive as this one. I think it was in an article in yesterday&#x2019;s <i>Herald</i> where a gentleman makes a very sensible observation: &#x2018;It is very unrealistic to have so many A&#x2019;s in English, when Sastri College, a prestige institution with all the best students in the country&#x2014;and a small number at that&#x2014;has so few A&#x2019;s. &#x2019; In the light of all this information we appear to be changing to the extent that we are descending &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>We are progressing. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Let us review the results of the last ten years, since Indian Education has become a department in its own right. If one considers the statistics and especially compares the 1987 results with those of the previous years, one will see that there is no correlation or consistency. This also reveals how unrealistic the hon members are. At least the hon the Minister of Education behaved in a very dignified way. I did not see him crowing: &#x2018;Look at our results!&#x2019; Nor did I ever see the Executive Director of Education doing this. What kind of defence do we want?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>I do not think that you read the newspapers properly.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>In fact, throughout the White regime they were never satisfied with the results. They said that these could improve. Let us be frank about it&#x2014;how do our results compare with those of the Whites in regard to quality? Why does the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council not make this demand for a unitary system of education and examinations?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Hear, hear!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>If there is no unitary system, do you know who you blame?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Quite often he even boasts that we can give service. Whose grandfather&#x2019;s money <span class="col_191-192" refersTo="page_0118"/>are we using to give service to the community? [Interjections.] They can go to the poor people. I tell you what, if I were to become chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council I would not don a garland. [Interjections.] I would not put on a garland and make a puppet of myself. He wants to put on a garland and pose for the TV, and it is shameful to see. [Interjections.] Over the past three months this question of housing has come up umpteen times but I ask you: What have we achieved?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>We will show you! We will show you in two months&#x2019; time. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I could cite numerous cases. I want to say something else. I have a great deal of respect for the other Ministers, irrespective of their inadequacies. We do not expect every one of them to be a super person. What I would like them to do, is to assert themselves as men.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>I respect the divinity in you. Many of your Ministers have said that you do not allow a single person &#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, this afternoon we have heard the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition speak on a motion of no confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council and especially in its Chairman. We have also listened to his counterpart, who said the very same thing. It rather surprised me. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council works 24 hours a day.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Twenty-six hours a day! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>He works 24 hours a day without sleeping because he has the community at heart. What do we get from the Official Opposition, however? Discredit. Why? If the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council were tomorrow to become leader of the Official Opposition, he should do the same thing as well. He should discredit the majority party. That is his privilege, but what sort of privilege are they using here? The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and the Ministers&#x2019; Council itself are doing a wonderful job.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Hear, hear!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>What has happened in our community between 1984 and 1987? Look back at the Inanda riots. Look back at what happened there. Where was our opposition then? [Interjections.] Where was our opposition? [Interjections.] Who was there? Who was calling us privately and saying: &#x201C;Call the Defence Force into the area&#x201D;? It was not the members of our party, Mr Chairman, but the members of the Opposition.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Where were you?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>That is a good question to ask. You go and ask the people of Isipingo where I was. We on this side of the House sacrificed our time while the intellectuals sat in their offices. We do the work. Give us some credit. I am asking for a little credit. Why discredit us? [Interjections.]</p>

<p>There are faults in any human being. We have made a few mistakes and we want to put those mistakes right. [Interjections.] I personally agree that there should be a commission of inquiry.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Hear, hear!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>As the hon member Mr Abram-Mayet has said, our hands are clean on this side. They are definitely clean. [Interjections.] When it comes to bribery and corruption, I can stand behind the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. There might be some maladministration. Some favouritism may take place. [Interjections.] We are only a political party and one or two individuals may well be promoted. We are only human beings.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Favouritism does take place.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>It does. Look at Mr P W Botha, the Leader of the National Party. Look at that component. Some of those unfortunate people who were promoted do not know anything about politics. Some are in the field. It is a political party running the country, not a private business.</p>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is a tireless worker.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Hear, hear!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>He works. To come back to the Inanda riots, he made sacrifices there. The Ministers&#x2019; Council went out of its way to see to it that homeless people were given a home. They went <span class="col_193-194" refersTo="page_0119"/>to the camps. We were at the camps. I was in the camp.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>No, Mr Chairman.</p>

<p>When the Natal flood disaster took place, I think I was the last man to leave Durban that night. I left Louis Botha Airport at five o&#x2019;clock that night and I arrived in Cape Town at two o&#x2019;clock in the morning. When we received news of the disaster, the hon the Minister of the Budget was the first man out in Natal. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and most of the members of our party were there immediately. I and my counterpart in Solidarity stayed in Cape Town. It was no fault of ours. There were no bookings for us and the airport was closed. It was therefore no fault of ours.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, I think it is definitely wrong of the Opposition to accuse us. Let us come to our senses. Our people need development, but even before development, they need good leadership, as the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition says. I agree with him. All these allegations should be completely removed from this Chamber. The only way we can get it out of this Chamber is to call for a commission of inquiry.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>Will you support it?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, I will support it. I will support it in my caucus; I will support it outside; I will support it everywhere. [Interjections.] Mr Chairman, if hon members know of any man that has stolen anything, they have police stations in their areas. They should go to the police station and open a charge of fraud, theft or whatever and let it be investigated. Such allegations are found in any part of the world. If there is an opposition party in Parliament, such allegations will be made.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Whose ideas are those? They are not yours. Who put them into your head? Tell us. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>With all respect to the hon member for Reservoir Hills, I speak here with sincerity. I do not play with words. I have no time to play with words. If the hon member for Reservoir Hills brings forward enough evidence against the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, I will stand by him and support it, whether outside this House or inside. That commission of inquiry must take place in order to clear our name. However, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council should be given some credit. Look at the development that is taking place. Look at our housing development. Look at our education development. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member take a simple question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I am not prepared to take any questions. If the hon member writes to me, I will reply.</p>

<p>I want to plead with the opposition parties to give this side of the House some credit. We are simple, hard-working people. We love our community, and when I say community, I do not mean only the Indian community. We love the various communities in this country. We want to upgrade every community and see them live in peace.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>What about Prospecton?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon member for Stanger speaks of Prospecton. I blame the House of Delegates for that. I want to be quite candid about it: I blame the House of Delegates. It was my party that was represented at the Demarcation Board; it was not Solidarity or the PRP. I want to be sincere. It was my own party&#x2019;s members that let us down in Prospecton. I am not going to go back on that.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, looking at problems, I want this House to have full confidence in the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Are you appealing?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#khan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">N E KHAN</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, I am appealing for that, because I know, and hon members know in their hearts, what calibre man he is. He works hard for the community. During the last five years the poor hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council never left this country. He loves his community and he stayed here. [Interjections.] This side of the House consists of worthy responsible people and I support the amendment of the hon member Mr Abram.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Before the hon member for Camperdown proceeds, I want to refer to the point of order raised by the hon member for Reservoir Hills concerning the statement made by the hon member Mr S Abram. Expressions are only unparliamentary <span class="col_195-196" refersTo="page_0120"/>when applied to an hon member or a speech by the hon member in the House and not when applied to policies and speeches made outside the House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#viyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J VIYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I stand up with a heavy heart this afternoon. I am perplexed by the attitude of some hon members in this House. One ought to be reminded that hon members of Parliament are very high office bearers in this country and it is needless to mention that hon members should conduct themselves with some dignity and decorum, rather than behave as though they are sitting in a bar, having a few beers all night long.</p>

<p>That being so, I stand up this afternoon to support the motion moved by the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition. It is not so much that he has been able to convince me, but the hon member Mr S Abram on 21 May 1987 convinced me beyond any shadow of doubt that the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council is a very nasty man. He has convinced me&#x2014;it is on record here&#x2014;together with a senior hon member of the other side who is absent today, namely the hon member for Natal Midlands. Between the two of them, they put up an unassailable case against the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. Nothing can erase it as it is recorded in Hansard: Delegates, vol 1, 18-22 May 1987. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I put a question to the hon member for Camperdown? I would like to put it to him that when the defectors from the NPP went to Solidarity they did not like the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. In order to get the approval of Villa Liza, they wanted a Ministers&#x2019; Council under the chairmanship of the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition. [Interjections.] My question is that under the leadership of the Opposition they would have approved&#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Will the hon member Mr Thaver please resume his seat?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I would like to quote from Hansard: Delegates, 20 May 1987, col 78. I quote as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">The decision to defect from the National Peoples Party was not taken lightly. It was done after months and months, in fact, years, of soul-searching, and attempts from within the National Peoples Party to rectify matters. It was done because there was so much interference in education that it was not possible for us to endure any longer. It was not possible for us to support and cover up the interference that was taking place.</block>

<p>The hon the Deputy Minister of Local Government, Housing and Agriculture made this statement which must not be disputed.</p>

<p>I have had no personal dealings with the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. I have not gone to his office to chat with him and I have had no tea and coffee with him but more than anything else I go by what is debated in this House. I place great value on members&#x2019; contributions in this House and members must bear it in mind that when they make statements they make them for the benefit of the public, not for the gallery.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>They make them because of the circumstances at the time.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I quote again from Hansard, col 81. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I put a question to the hon member?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>No, Sir, I have a very short time available to me and I want to make my contribution by dealing with the matters I have here. I am not fabricating facts here, I am quoting from the official record of the South African Parliament. If hon members want to dispute me they must dispute the records of Hansard. Do not dispute with me please. If you have to sit with grasshoppers and tortoises. Then do so by all means. I quote from col 81:</p>

<block name="quote">What was the end result of my efforts? I was propping up an individual who was bestowing upon himself powers out of all proportion to the original intent.</block>

<p>I am referring to the Chairman of the Minister&#x2019;s Council that he is unreliable, not trustworthy and irresponsible. [Interjections.] May I continue Sir?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I think the interjections are getting a little out of hand. May I ask hon members to give members an opportunity to continue their speeches. When hon members reply they will have the opportunity to make the necessary replies.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#viyman">

<from><span class="col_197-198" refersTo="page_0121"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J VIYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I want to continue quoting from col 81:</p>

<block name="quote">When I was tramping the length and breadth of this country, pleading for support for this party, I told people to join because this party was a vehicle for the upliftment of the people of the country. We would attempt to undo the wrongs of the past and see that justice be done. What was the end result of my efforts? I was propping up an individual who was bestowing upon himself powers out of all proportion to the original intent.</block>

<p>On that statement alone I fully support the motion of no-confidence in the participation of the hon the Chairman of the Minister&#x2019;s Council. This is an indictment by his own members, it is not my statement. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Are you prepared to walk out?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Walk out! All walk out and I shall follow!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Are you prepared to follow him now?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I am not following just anybody. This is on record; it is indisputably there. No member who has spoken has disputed or refuted those allegations. They therefore stand as the truth. [Interjections.] That member is an honourable person. I firmly believe that he spoke the truth in his speech. He poured his heart out. I can accept that. I am of the opinion that in the interests of the whole of South Africa the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council should step down. It has been proved by his own party members that he is not a worthy person for that post.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Can you tell them what you told them three weeks ago?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I go to col 83 where Mr S Pachai says:</p>

<block name="quote">How was I expected to continue along those lines? The interests of the people were not being served. The interests of the country were not being served by the autocratic behaviour of the leader of the party.</block>

<p>These are not my allegations. Sir I am sorry to have to state this publicly but they are not my allegations. It is my belief that the hon member for Natal Midlands spoke the truth, otherwise he would not have been elevated to the post of Deputy Minister. I cannot see the reason why when a person makes such statements he is promoted. After he made those allegations, to a Deputy Minister! There must therefore be some truth in the statement. He has been promoted out of fear of further exposure. That is the logical conclusion at which any sane person will arrive. Why is a person who has made such damaging statements in Parliament and in public, which have been placed on record for all time, subsequently promoted? To keep his mouth shut he was given a carrot to chew.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>In an anonymous pamphlet in Tongaat the same sentence was used.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>If you want to become a Deputy Minister, come to this side!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I would like to go further and address the hon member Mr Abram. In col 156 of Hansard 21 May 1987 he expressed great respect for the hon the Minister of Education and Culture. I too have great respect for him. He is a capable, sincere and dedicated person. I agree fully with what the hon member Mr S Abram said. He said:</p>

<block name="quote">The hon the Minister of Education and Culture, for whom I have the greatest respect, is one of those men on that side for whom I shall never lose my respect.</block>

<p>However, he immediately goes on to say:</p>

<block name="quote">That hon Minister is only a proxy in that Ministers&#x2019; Council.</block>

<p>This substantiates the hon the Deputy Minister&#x2019;s statement that there is interference in the field of education.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Do you know the meaning of the word proxy?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>All Ministers are proxies of the hon the State President. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I advise certain hon members on that side of the House that they have ample time to look at their copies of Hansard. If they do not have copies, I shall lend them mine. They can go through their Hansards and reply in due course. [Interjections.]</p>

<p><span class="col_199-200" refersTo="page_0122"/>Call this recording a lie! Call the recording incorrect and then you are man enough!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Did we come here for reading or a lesson?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#viyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J VIYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>This is not a lesson. I am stating my belief and my support for the most important motion before this House. I have reason to support this and I am proving it. I am not afraid to do so.</p>

<p>In col 159 the hon member Mr S Abram said the following:</p>

<block name="quote">Mr Chairman, I want to place on record that I am pleased the hon member for Laudium is here. I know of the pain and suffering he has experienced. I think this hon member actually confirms that there is something gravely wrong in this administration.</block>

<p>This motion before the House today is one of no confidence. We hear this from a senior member of the ruling party. We have to believe this because the person in these pages is the Chairman of Committees.</p>

<p>I highly value that statement. The hon member Mr Abram says (Hansard: Delegates, 21 May 1987, col 180):</p>

<block name="quote">I accept that my friend, the hon member for Laudium, is definitely aware of certain information. If he brings this into the open it could have far-reaching repercussions.</block>

<p>On the other hand, if that is not the truth I think the hon Mr Abram should not retain his chairmanship of committees. If it was an untruth, if it was a lie, why is that hon member &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Freedom of speech. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Because of the truth. [Interjections.] They feared further accusations concerning those problems. That information could have repercussions that would affect the entire tricameral Parliament. This reflects on the general Cabinet.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Why do you not comment on what the hon member Mr Abram said today?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>It is a pity he ran away. I gave him fair warning that I was going to tackle him, but he left us.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Introduce some new dimensions to the debate.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I am coming to that. If the hon member Mr Thaver will keep quiet I shall come to it. [Interjections.] He interferes, and as soon as there is a problem, he burns his fingers.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Come to this side, and we will give you a Deputy Ministership.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I want to disclose to this House that I have been consulting with a colleague of mine for the past few days. Yesterday in the early hours of the morning, at about six o&#x2019;clock, a high-ranking public servant telephoned that number. When I answered the telephone it was a certain very high-ranking public servant based in Durban who said that he wanted to speak to Mr So-and-So. I said that that gentleman was in the bathroom. To that he replied that he could speak to me as well. These are the words he used:</p>

<block name="quote">Whatever Raj may be I think we should back him up. I think we should not proceed with this No-confidence motion. You guys should have another think about this.</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>That is hearsay evidence, man.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Hearsay evidence recorded in Hansard?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>That is hearsay!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Hearsay, and recorded in Hansard? That was reported on the front page of the <i>Sunday Tribune</i> yesterday. The report indicates a high-ranking officer who is alleged to have induced people to act incorrectly. This short article in yesterday&#x2019;s newspaper fully implicates a high-ranking official in the Department of Education. I have to believe the hon member Mr S Abram-Mayet. That being the question, I was surprised at his attitude today. I think he has gone wrong somewhere, or else he himself is out of touch in the conclusions he draws.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Do you believe sensational items in the newspapers?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Either that hon member stands by what he has sworn to, or else he goes to jail for perjury. That is cut and dried.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Somebody is going to go to jail for perjury.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from><span class="col_201-202" refersTo="page_0123"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>Introduce a new dimension into the debate. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#viyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J VIYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I am coming to that. In column 162 the hon member Mr S Abram says the following:</p>

<block name="quote">Is this the level to which the Ministers&#x2019; Council has sunk? It is time for it to be dissolved as a matter of honour. It should go.</block>

<p>Therefore, these are the words that entice me to support the motion before the House. Having heard the arguments produced by the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition, coupled with this proof from Hansard, I have no choice.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>If you believe in them so much, are you prepared to follow them now? They were brain washed in that room.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>The hon Mr Abram said (Hansard: Delegates, 21 May 1987, col 162):</p>

<block name="quote">I was given a lollipop for 18 months. That administration was to help resolve the manifold housing problems of my constituents but in the past 18 months nothing has happened.</block>

<p>May I quote what the hon member said just a little further on? He said: After what has been happening and the misgivings expressed by many hon members, how they can, with a clear conscience, remain over there.</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, I would like to question the hon Mr Abram about this today. On the 21 May 1987 he begged, pleaded and beseeched the members of the ruling party&#x2014;after building a very strong case&#x2014;to cross over. The hon Mr Abram continued on that same page saying that what they are doing by remaining there is politically propping up an individual whose actions show very clearly that his own welfare comes first and not that of the community that he purports to serve. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Why did you leave Solidarity, yet now you are supporting them?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, please may I have some injury time here? The hon member Mr Thaver really wants me to react to his purile interjections. They bear no reference to anything! His interjections do not concern any evidence that I have given here, Sir.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>It is hurting you.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>It is not hurting me, but hon members are wasting my time. [Interjections ] Today I would like to question the hon Mr Abram&#x2019;s defence of the Ministers&#x2019; Council&#x2014; especially since about a year ago he came here with a very strong indictment against the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and against the Ministers&#x2019; Council as such. That was a very strong indictment which I just quoted. How can he get up there and say these things today?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>He is an honourable man!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>It is a dishonourable deed, done by an honourable man.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>He is Brutus. Et tu?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I question his sincerity, his loyalty &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Why are you standing upon crutches against me? Please stand on your own two feet.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, an hon member of the Opposition in an interjection has just referred to an hon member from this side as brutus. This, I submit, is unparliamentary.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Will the hon member please inform me as to exactly who said so?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#baig">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M Y BAIG</person>:</from>

<p>It was the hon member for Phoenix, Mr Chairman.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Did the hon member for Phoenix make that interjection referring to the hon member as &#x201C;Brutus&#x201D;?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I said: &#x201C;Et tu, Brute?&#x201D;. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Does the hon member admit that he referred to an hon member?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>I admit having said so, Mr Chairman.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Will the hon member withdraw it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>I withdraw it, Sir. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>And apologise!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! I <span class="col_203-204" refersTo="page_0124"/>must tell the hon member Mr Thaver that the Chair will decide whether or not hon members should apologise.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#thaver">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M THAVER</person>:</from>

<p>I want to apologise to you, Sir, but&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Will the hon member resume his seat, please.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#viyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J VIYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I was on the point of emphasizing the hon member Mr Abram&#x2019;s defence of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, which was rather shallow and hollow. It had no substance. [Interjections.] He had no reason to attack any individual members on this side of the House.</p>

<p>Furthermore, he did not respond to the motion. He did not address the motion before the House but launched a personal attack. It was a case of the kettle calling the pot black.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Do not use him as a crutch!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I am not using him as a crutch.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Come forward with your own arguments. I will face you.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>But those are my arguments. I never run away. I quote further from Hansard as follows:</p>

<block name="quote">If this were not so national strategies would already have been submitted to this House. Those plans would have been put into action. Instead, what do we have? We have this type of ham-handedness, abuse of political power and blackmail to which the hon member for North Coast has been subjected at all times.</block>

<p>I do not know what that is, Sir.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_the_budget">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF THE BUDGET</person>:</from>

<p>Tell us something new, man!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>It is not new? [Interjections.] This is something new. This goes to prove &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>That sentiment is outdated.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>I am coming to something. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council must be reasonable. The hon member Mr Abram went on to say:</p>

<block name="quote">This House has no option but to see that this Ministers&#x2019; Council is removed.</block>

<p>I do not know where his loyalty lies today&#x2014; whether he is sincere when he talks about his interest in the welfare of the South African population. Last year he asked for the removal of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and today he defends the Ministers&#x2019; Council. I suppose that little dance was performed by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council something like this: &#x201C;Villa Lisa!, Villa Lisa!, Villa Lisa!, Villa Lisa!&#x201D;. [Interjections.] Yes, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council performed very well there on that day. He rhymed his words, and his tone was very good. [Interjections.] The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council said:</p>

<block name="quote">Why is it that somebody is blocking every other area &#x2026;</block>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon member says that that is recorded in column 226. Could he show us where the dance is recorded? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Unfortunately, the dance is missing and so I filled it in for you, Sir. [Interjections.] I learned something new that day. [Interjections.] I am indebted to him for that. I learned something new on 22 May 1987. I quote again from Hansard (Delegates, 22 May 1987, col 226):</p>

<block name="quote">Why is it that somebody is blocking every other area we are examining? Who is doing this?</block>

<p>That was the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council&#x2019;s reply to the hon member Mr Abram. I quote further:</p>

<block name="quote">They are eating Villa Lisa like a lollipop. They are going beserk&#x2014;Villa Lisa! Villa Lisa! Mona Lisa! Nothing but Villa Lisa! The chairman of the management committee of Boksburg told me that fantastic commissions are being offered to people just to have it declared Indian.</block>

<p>[Interjections.] He goes on to say:</p>

<block name="quote">The hon member for Natal Midlands confided in me that the person who took the option to buy these properties is none other than the hon member Mr S Abram.</block>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council himself&#x2014;I want to use a word here, but it is unparliamentary, so I will use it in my own thoughts&#x2014;is submitted to this pressure. Because I could not solve the jigsaw puzzle, I was surprised why, after making such strong statements, <span class="col_205-206" refersTo="page_0125"/>the hon member Mr Abram defended the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council and in fact launched a wild attack on the Official Opposition, its leader and all its members. He even made a statement to the effect that Solidarity will stop existing in the Transvaal and will never exist there again.</p>

<p>Going back to the reply of the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council concerning the naming of persons who were going to go before the commission, I am led to believe that &#x2026; [Time expired.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister_of_local_government_and_agriculture">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER OF LOCAL GOVERNMENT AND AGRICULTURE</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, one could write a thesis on no-confidence motions in this House from its very inception in 1985. When we came here, during the first session in 1985, even before an hon Minister could find his office, there was a no-confidence motion. In 1986 there was a calm &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>It is parliamentary convention, you know.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>We understand conventions, but I am talking about circumstances.</p>

<p>In 1986 there was a calm, because the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition was sitting in the Ministers&#x2019; Council. In 1987 we also witnessed a vicious motion against the Ministers&#x2019; Council. Coming to 1988, we find the no-confidence motion is now compounded, adding the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, and it even goes further. One begins to wonder. Naturally we are approaching the tail end of the five-year session. I am beginning to wonder what motion will be submitted in 1989. Perhaps it will be a motion to say that we have tried and tried, we have come to the end of the road, we might as well submit a motion of no confidence in our own party.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#viyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J VIYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon the Minister take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>Oh yes, the hon member is a good friend of mine.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I would like the hon the Minister to tell this House whether it is not parliamentary convention that the opposition parties are afforded an opportunity, immediately after Parliament opens, to assess the shortcomings of the functioning of the ruling party during the previous year, by way of a motion of no confidence. [Interjections.] Secondly, is this motion moved only in this House, or in all three Houses of Parliament?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, the hon member for Camperdown has answered his own question. He made some utterances here this afternoon with regard to the hon the Deputy Minister of Local Government, Housing and Agriculture, Mr Salem Abram-Mayet, as well as the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. We had witnessed, as a result of human nature&#x2014;no one will deny that&#x2014;the attitude of the White regime in South Africa and its rigidity towards apartheid and discrimination. However, what happened when they decided to move towards reform? Notwithstanding the utterances of both these colleagues of mine, there has been a change of heart with the passage of time. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Whatever it was, every day is a new day. Every individual has the right to decide for himself what is good and where he can best serve his community. This House has to understand that we have 45 hon members here representing 800 000 people outside, irrespective of their attitude towards the House of Delegates and whether they subscribe to it or not. Our utterances, behaviour, approach and our debates in this House will have an effect in one way or another on the well-being of these people as a whole.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>A detrimental effect!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>Whether it is a detrimental effect or not depends on the attitude of individuals and their values in society.</p>

<p>We have no hidden agendas in our whole exercise.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#rajab">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M RAJAB</person>:</from>

<p>What are you trying to say?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member for Springfield asks me what point I am trying to make. He has to follow up the motion that was put before this House.</p>

<p>Keeping in mind the comments made by the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition as well as the comment of the hon the Chairman of the House about his deep concern with regard to the dignity and decorum in this House, the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition could not have said anything more appropriate than that we should for once and for all lay the prevailing attitude to rest. [Interjections.] These issues must be buried for once and for all as it does not auger <span class="col_207-208" refersTo="page_0126"/>well for the dignity and the image of the Indian community.</p>

<p>The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council announced on 29 January 1988 that he will request the hon the State President to appoint a judicial committee of inquiry, once the hon member for Reservoir Hills repeats the 1985 allegation &#x2026; [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I also want to mention another statement made here this afternoon by the hon member Mr S Abram. Can a person be more brave than he was in stating here before hon members in this House that, should there be any reflection on him with regard to his transaction, he would be prepared to surrender whatever he has in life?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>No, Mr Chairman.</p>

<p>The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition spoke about teachers&#x2019; rights and their leave to participate. We should be sensitive in handling this matter and we should give the teaching fraternity the respectability that they deserve. Theirs is a noble profession, and it is not the right of anyone in authority to deprive them of their political rights. Does their belonging to the teaching fraternity necessarily mean that they are not supposed to participate in party politics? No teacher would leave his or her classroom to partake in party political activities without prior consent.</p>

<p>It is unfortunate that the national chairman of the Solidarity Party should say that Solidarity pledges to fire teachers who were promoted for political reasons. I quote:</p>

<block name="quote">Teachers who were promoted for political reasons faced the &#x2018;boot&#x2019; if the Solidarity Party takes control of the House of Delegates. This warning was issued by Solidarity&#x2019;s national chairman, Mr Ismail Omar, in campaign speeches for the by-election to be contested between Solidarity and the National Peoples Party in the Eastern Transvaal this week.</block>

<p>This appeared in the <i>Sunday Times Extra</i> of 31 January 1988. I ask this with all humility and with all due respect. I mentioned earlier that the teaching profession is a noble one. We are dependent on the teaching fraternity to mold future generations of any nation. However, for the head of a political party to make a statement of this nature is most unbecoming and it is unforgivable. On the other hand, the Teachers&#x2019; Association of South Africa&#x2014;and I want to quote from the <i>Post</i> of November 1987&#x2014;state:</p>

<block name="quote">Teachers are being forced &#x2018;against their wishes&#x2019; to canvass votes for the two candidates standing for the Tongaat Parliamentary by-election. <br/>President of the Teachers&#x2019; Association of South Africa, Pat Samuels, said several teachers had complained but they could not act until they had received affidavits.</block>

<p>He confirms that teachers were participating in both political parties, not necessarily only one.</p>

<p>So much for the participation of teachers. As far as the Tongaat by-election is concerned, it has brought about great knowledge within our community. One of the things that appeared in the manifesto of the Solidarity Party was the Greyland fiasco. I would like to know what the Greyland fiasco is all about.</p>

<p>When we reduce anything to a manifesto and state that it is the Greyland fiasco, what does one expect a simple man to understand by that when he is dependent on one as the representative who has to find a solution to the problem? On the one hand you have a housing crisis in a region. The White authority naturally will plan housing within an Indian area on whatever land is available. That is what they will do. On the other hand, they plan to build houses within an agricultural area. We were not responsible for that.</p>

<p>We were in fact taken into Tongaat by our late hon member. We were not the architects of Greylands, but when there was a reaction and when there was such a political rumpus in the community with regard to Greylands we naturally had to take action&#x2014;and the necessary action&#x2014;on the basis that we cannot allow the Indian agricultural community to lose any further land. Therefore I, being responsible for agriculture, in communication with the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council in the House of Delegates said that that land of Greylands is not negotiable. It was not our intention to take the decision which we had taken as far as Greylands is concerned during the election period. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Because of the vacuum that was being created in that region and because of the concern of those 31 farmers in that area this Ministers&#x2019; Council went out of their way to find a solution and in so doing solved the problem leaving the land that belonged <span class="col_209-210" refersTo="page_0127"/>to the 31 farmers intact. The problems are created as a result of the circumstances, the shortage of homes for the Indian community.</p>

<p>What does a White authority do? A White authority will not go beyond the parameters of an Indian area to find land. This decision we have taken to see to it that Greylands remains in tact. I therefore cannot understand the reference to the Greylands fiasco which appeared in the manifesto.</p>

<p>Another thing which is very sad&#x2014;and I want to say this very sincerely&#x2014;is that here was a news flash in the Tongaat by-election. He wants Tamil, Telegu and Moslem voters to vote for the majority party. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I want people to search their consciences when this information is distributed in public. [Interjections.] The news flash from Tongaat blatantly brings about language differences that we fight every day in this country. If we have utterances &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_members">

<from><person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBERS</person>:</from>

<p>Who printed that?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>Ask yourselves who printed it. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#iyman">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">J V IYMAN</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I would like to ask the hon the Minister a question.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Is the hon the Minister prepared to take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon the Minister is not prepared to take a question.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>I want to tell the hon member for Stanger that because of the fact that there had been a news flash related to the elections, it was their responsibility &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>I dissociated myself from that! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member for Camperdown also made some statements here with regard the indictment against the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. Here again no one is guilty unless he is found guilty. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has made his stand very, very clear.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>The post box has vanished now.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>There has been open-heartedness here and the voluntary calling for certain commissions of inquiry. It is therefore our responsibility, taking into account the needs of the people in this country, to continue the work. This Ministers&#x2019; Council works literally seven days a week, doing their utmost in one of the most difficult periods of transition in South Africa. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>When it came to the flood issue the hon member for Isipingo made a strong point. He spoke from the heart here. People should also assess what role they played during the flood disaster in Natal. When the floods struck Natal, they did not take into account party politics, colour or creed; it had a devastating effect on the whole of Natal.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Should we be mixing party politics in an issue like that? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>I want to remind the hon member for Reservoir Hills that disaster does not choose colour, caste or creed.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>So should the flood have taken sides?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>It is therefore imperative that every person &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>&#x2026; should do his duty.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>&#x2026; should do his utmost. [Interjections.] It is all very well for hon members to make statements here, but they should also get out into the field and feel the heat of the sun. They should go and see for themselves the devastating effects of the floods. [Interjections.] There has been condemnation and a motion of total no-confidence in the Ministers&#x2019; Council.</p>

<p>However, those people who require service, who look forward to leadership from every angle to provide their requirements, acknowledge with appreciation and thanks what has been done. We understand that there is recognition in some quarters for the strides that have been made in many facets of life within the Indian community. In <i>Daily News</i> of November 30 1987 they again acknowledge in a message from Tongaat that what is undeniably apparent from the outcome of the Tongaat Parliamentary by-election is that many more people cast their vote than in any other previous election.</p>

<p>Equally apparent, however, is that the voters <span class="col_211-212" refersTo="page_0128"/>prefer not to be seen doing it. The extraordinarily high number of special votes&#x2014;4840&#x2014;seems to indicate that most people did not want to go to the polling booth in person. The best explanation for this rather unusual feature is that it was caused by the fierce boycott campaign that was waged.</p>

<p>The NPP fought a hard and tough campaign in a sometimes rather unpleasant election battle, and is pleased with the result despite calumniation and opposition from all sides. The fact remains that the NPP candidate, Mr A Hurbans, who lost with 272 votes in August 1984, this time polled 3533 votes, and the percentage poll moved up from 10% to 38,6%. This is a significant increase. It amounts to a snub to the boycott and points clearly to a willingness to participate in the tricameral system.</p>

<p>The <i>Daily News</i> cordially acknowledges, not the person or the names, but the work and the goods that have been delivered. No one can stand up, however much that they want to find fault and say that there has been no work, that the community at large is dissatisfied. Therefore, even insofar as the Opposition is concerned, it is imperative for them to understand the needs of the people. I have always said that our approach to matters must be dependent on the circumstances. Therefore our brand of politics and our approach should percolate right down to the masses, to the poorer section of the population.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>That would be absolutely dangerous.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>This is what we should be doing.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>That is absolutely dangerous if that is your level of politics.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>The Leader of the Official Opposition says that that is dangerous. What I am merely trying to point out is that whilst you are looking at ideals and new directions, let us give importance to those people who have been stagnating for 128 years. That is equally important.</p>

<p>However, we cannot afford the luxury of politicising, be it conventional or otherwise. At the end of the day this has a detrimental effect on the people whom we serve in this country. With this I want to say that we are quite satisfied with the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, who is a Trojan at work, who literally works around the clock, who makes every effort possible. He is a human being. Give him the cooperation he needs.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodliar">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">C N MOODLIAR</person>:</from>

<p>Are you following his example?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#minister">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">MINISTER</person>:</from>

<p>Notwithstanding all the utterances, all that is reduced to newspapers and the media. Let there be cooperation and let us look ahead and support the amendment to the motion submitted by my colleague the hon Mr Abram.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Our junior member.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>I would rather answer that first, Mr Chairman. I have heard these snide remarks about being a junior member, being a baby, being a kid. I think I must make it very clear that dynamite comes in small packages. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I will not comment on that now, Mr Chairman, but a little later. I have no Hansard in front of me from which I can quote, because I am disappointed. I would have expected a fiery Motion of No Confidence from the Opposition. [Interjections.] Mr Chairman, God gave man two ears and one mouth, so that he can hear twice as much as he can speak. I would like certain hon members to pay attention to that fact.</p>

<p>I go along with the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition when he speaks of negative publicity in the news media. He also mentioned certain things which are recorded in Hansard. Being a member of the ruling party of this House&#x2014;that is the NPP in case there is any doubt in anybody&#x2019;s mind&#x2014;if I am not mistaken Hansard also recorded the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition as stating last year that his party would boycott Parliament this year. We are waiting. This is February. Furthermore, I have noticed in the Press certain sensational headlines about the so-called swim by the National Chairman of Solidarity. That swim also swam away. [Interjections.] The National Peoples Party does not believe in sensational headlines. It is consistent in its thinking in approaching its local, provincial and national goals. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, one notes that this is the first major speech being made by the hon member and I think it is <span class="col_213-214" refersTo="page_0129"/>traditional for other members to pay attention. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member may continue.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, it would appear as if the Opposition has based its arguments on appeasement combined with an obsession to gain control of the administration of the House of Delegates. This is the impression I get.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>That is your impression.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>That is the correct impression. When officials of a political party cannot correctly fill in a prescribed nomination form, much is left to be desired. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>They talk lies!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>I am of the opinion &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council has just used the words: &#x201C;They talk lies&#x201D;.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>The officials of the party&#x2014;the Transvaal officials&#x2014;they spoke lies. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I am of the opinion that the Transvaal Secretary of Solidarity has intentionally misled the public and the Nomination Court by incorrectly filling in section B4 of the prescribed Nomination Form in terms of Act 42 of 1979, an Act &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I would like to ask the hon member why he is talking about someone who is not present. Would he be prepared to repeat this outside so that this man can deal with it adequately? Please repeat those names that you are using here under the privilege of Parliament outside.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I did not call anyone names. I merely stated, that the forms were incorrectly filled in.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>Lots of people make mistakes.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>One cannot make a mistake like that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>In terms of Act 42 of 1979 and the amended Electoral Act, Act 45 of 1984, that particular section ought to have been filled in by a sitting member of Parliament. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>How can you pass judgment when the magistrate was there? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>The magistrate was acting merely as a presiding officer. He was designated by the Department of Home Affairs to act as such. [Interjections.] Why we did not go to court is another matter. We took senior counsel&#x2019;s opinion, but the NPP decided to demonstrate a measure of dignity and to contest that election by the ballot-box.</p>

<p>This is the dignity! We have contested that election via the ballot box and we have proved that we have the support and confidence of the electorate in the Eastern Transvaal. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>The hon member for Camperdown referred to the appointment of the hon the Deputy Minister, but no one has referred to the appointment of a member of the Official Opposition as an MEC in the Transvaal, something which was done purely on merit. [Interjections.] That was done purely on merit. However, nobody commends the Ministers&#x2019; Council. Whether the Official Opposition likes it or not every hon Minister seated on the Ministers&#x2019; Council is as much our Minister as theirs. [Interjections.] They are their Ministers as well.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>That is why we have a say.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>You have a say if you use the proper channels. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>The National Peoples Party does not waffle and does not backtrack. We believe we must be consistent in our thinking.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>That is exactly what we are asking you for!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>I agree with the hon the Leader of the Official Opposition that we must not have adverse publicity which gives this House a negative attitude.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>But who deliberately flogs that horse?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">
