<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>We could talk about this until the cows come home. However, I shall limit myself to the time I have allocated to me and debate this particular aspect. I was rather disturbed at people <span class="col_215-216" refersTo="page_0130"/>quoting extensively from Hansard. I can appreciate that the results of the Eastern Transvaal by-election may be a bitter pill for Solidarity to swallow. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>The hon member Mr Akoob did very well. All his family is there.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>You helped him privately.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, I helped him. What does that have to do with you? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>I also want to take a look at the track record of the NPP. We must give credit where it is due. Let us look at the housing sphere. We have made great strides in housing during the past year.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Grave strides!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>I said great strides. [Interjections.] We have made great strides in education despite the cries that there are crises in education.</p>

<p>Agriculture is another area we could look at.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Under this Minister, we have produced.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>We have produced! [Interjections.] We have made great strides in respect of health and welfare and pension services. All these serve as proof of the NPP&#x2019;s solid track record. [Interjections.] Surely some credit must be given for the positive initiatives that have been taken by the NPP.</p>

<p>A colleague of mine mentioned earlier that we are human, and we are. I want to endorse that. We are only human, and we do err. I want to mention that both my primary school and high school teachers are seated in the opposition party and I have great respect for those gentlemen.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>What you are saying must be the truth, because they taught you to say that. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>What I am saying, is that the NPP can be compared &#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>To the National Party!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>No, it can be compared to the milk powder that is advertised on TV because we are always on top. We are not inside our offices. In fact, during our election campaign we were compared to a giant chain store because a promise we make is a promise we keep, too.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Just do not get checkmated, that is all. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>The NPP has been producing results. As much as we dislike the tricameral system, the NPP&#x2019;s policy has always been to use and exploit every avenue and every forum to air our grievances, and this is exactly what we are doing because we believe that this is the highest level at which one can debate. It is no good our making a noise outside and shouting slogans, like the extraparliamentary forces.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#bhana">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R BHANA</person>:</from>

<p>And running to the Press.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, and running to the Press. [Interjections.] We want to come here and say to everyone who wants to debate with us: &#x201C;Please come here. This is the highest forum for debate. Sit opposite us and talk. Let us debate it here because this is the place on which the world focuses its attention&#x201D;. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I want to talk about the role played by the extra-parliamentary forces in the recent by-election. They have come out with very strongly-worded pamphlets against the tricameral Parliament. I simply want to say that we do not believe in just going to the communities during election time. I want to give the opposition its due credit also, because they have also done some work in their constituencies. However, the extra-parliamentary forces only go to the electorate when there is an election in an area. Other than that, they do not go there. That is the time they expect to gain support from the communities they purport to represent. The Eastern Transvaal byelection stretches over some 17 000 square kilometres covering 33 towns and we have had the opportunity of addressing public meetings in about 19 of these. These were open public meetings, they were not closed, and we allowed question times. The hon member for Stanger must take note the fact that we allowed question time for anybody, including the extra-parliamentary forces, to come forward and put their questions to us.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, may I ask the hon member whether he is aware of the fact that we also allow question time. Why this reference? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from><span class="col_217-218" refersTo="page_0131"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I am specifically referring to a meeting held in Kinross. This was a public meeting which members of the National Peoples Party attended. I will demonstrate what was done. All the papers were picked up, the time was looked at and the meeting was declared closed. That is what happened in Kinross and what I am referring to. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Mr Chairman, all I am saying, is that most of the hon members seated in this House support the National Peoples Party and this motion which appears on the Order Paper appears to single out the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council. [Interjections.] It does. It does appear to single him out, otherwise it would not have said &#x201C;especially the Chairman&#x201D;. However, the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council who has headed the NPP all these years has produced results. If anybody has any illusions that they will try and throw a spanner in the works, or try to tear members from the NPP, I think they have a wrong conception, or they have miscalculated their arguments.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chetty">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K CHETTY</person>:</from>

<p>We have no intention of that. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>Oh yes, they have. They have miscalculated this whole thing, because the NPP is a party that is not based on opportunism. [Interjections.] It is a party that has vision, direction and is pragmatic. The NPP, as I indicated earlier on, has a positive track record of achievements. We do not have to go and look at Hansard and say: &#x201C;Listen, this is what has been said last year, or the year before.&#x201D;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>You cannot find it there.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>It is there. The evidence is there in reality. All hon members have to do is go and have a look at it. It is there, and it will always be there, because those monuments are built by virtue of the efforts put in by this Ministers&#x2019; Council, as much as members of the opposition parties would not like to admit it. It is all very well taking one&#x2019;s stand here, but privately members of the opposition parties adopt different stances and go to our Ministers&#x2019; offices and play a different tune altogether.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Our Ministers or State Ministers?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>I said &#x201C;our Ministers&#x201D;.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>You are talking about opposition members and &#x201C;our Ministers&#x201D;. How do you reconcile that?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>The members of the opposition parties have been going to the Ministers&#x2019; Council and to individual hon Ministers with their requests. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I would like to ask the hon member whether he believes that hon Ministers on the other side, who happen to be members of the National Peoples Party, are only to serve members of that party. Have we not got the same right, because the taxpayers&#x2019; money is being used to serve the community? What does he mean by saying that hon members from this side have been going to hon Ministers on that side? We will continue going. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>That is an observation, not a question. I am not saying that hon members of the Opposition should not go. I qualified that initially when I started speaking and I said that as much as hon members of the Opposition do not want to admit it, they are their hon Ministers as well. [Interjections.] That is what I said in my opening statement. [Interjections.] They are welcome to go to the hon the Ministers, because they are their Ministers as well. I am not denying them that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moodley">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">K MOODLEY</person>:</from>

<p>It is not a privilege, it is our right.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>It is not your money.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#shah">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">M S SHAH</person>:</from>

<p>An MP&#x2019;s salary consists of the taxpayers&#x2019; money as well.</p>

<p>In closing I want to say that the Ministers&#x2019; Council, consisting of hon NPP members, enjoys the full support in this House and also outside, because the electorate in the Eastern Transvaal has proven it. An increase of 519% from the 1984 results is no mean feat by any measure.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I rise to speak at a very awkward time. It is the last hour of today&#x2019;s debate, and I was sitting here hoping that there would be another speaker from the Opposition benches but I am now told that they have run out of hot air. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>This is the start of the fourth year that we are here. For the past three years we have traversed the same ground basically of internecine strife, of <span class="col_219-220" refersTo="page_0132"/>political point scoring, and of allegations and counter-allegations. I know that there are many political idiots in the Opposition benches but they do not have to prove it by opening their mouths. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, on a point of order: The hon member referred to the political idiots of the Opposition benches. Is that not unparliamentary?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! Did the hon member Mr Nowbath make the statement as alleged by the hon member for Stanger?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I made the statement that they are political idiots, not simply idiots. In that I am quoting Lenin. He himself referred to people who are not idiots, but political idiots. There is a big difference.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! So the hon member did refer to the hon members of the Opposition as political idiots?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Yes, Sir.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_house">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE HOUSE</person>:</from>

<p>Order! The hon member must please withdraw that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I will have to submit to your ruling. I withdraw it.</p>

<p>There are many hon members in the Opposition benches who do not have sufficient political know-how. They cannot think in political terms but only in terms of local parochial interest and nothing more than that. That is why they are not capable of carrying any constituency with them. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>I would like to clear up a certain matter. The hon the Leader of the Official Opposition has had a long and chequered career in the history of our people.</p>

<p>I have known him for possibly 20 years or more, during which time he was active in politics in the South African Indian Council. I knew him when he was a junior schoolboy, junior to me at school. I knew him in the days when, by his own admission, he grew up the hard way. He told me that as a young man, he did carry the bhanga and he did sell bananas. He did not, in fact, push any wheelbarrows. The bhanga belonged to the family and the bananas grew on the family farm, and he worked hard for it.</p>

<p>As a result of that background, he developed an interest in the question of jobs for our young people. There are two achievements that I should like to recall to this House.</p>

<p>Firstly, he was responsible for opening up the Transvaal as far as job opportunities for our young people are concerned. The fact that there are today possibly several possibilities for young men and young women with their families in the Transvaal, is due to him. The fact that today there are several hundred people in employment in the new Republic Bank is due to him. He is the man who pushed for this new Republic Bank&#x2014; the man who pushed for an Indian bank. Granted, he speaks in terms of what you call macro economics.</p>

<p>The new Republic Bank was taken over by the Whites; it is now under the control of the Standard Bank. However, it is an avenue of work. Towards the early part of the seventies he left politics on a crest of glory.</p>

<p>Despite that, he made a mistake in returning. He should never have returned to politics, for the simple reason that his heart is not in it. With the hon the Chairman, he is genuinely concerned for the community. However, he has been hijacked by people who have their own interests at heart. I must draw attention to this fact. He is, and has become, unfortunately, like a heavyweight boxer who seeks to make a comeback. When heavyweight boxers make a comeback they are battered and bruised. I say so having considered his motions of no-confidence over the past few years. They have never been the same. They have been adjusted to suit his needs and possibly he himself has had to concede to his colleagues.</p>

<p>Thus, the motion of no confidence at the beginning of 1985 was such a motion. He has been riding two horses at the same time. As I said, he made the mistake of coming back into politics, although his heart is not in it.</p>

<p>That is why we find that he does not really come across when he makes a speech, except at one point this afternoon when in a situation of emotional crisis he said: &#x201C;My heart bleeds for the Indians&#x201D;</p>

<p>That was a cry from the heart. Unfortunately, as I said, however, politics is not a game to be played by the faint-hearted, particularly people who have retired. It would have been better had he stayed in retirement.</p>

<p><span class="col_221-222" refersTo="page_0133"/>There has been a very vigorous call by members of the Opposition for a judicial commission of inquiry.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Two of your Ministers asked for that.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>If the hon member for Reservoir Hills had noted very carefully, he would have known that our members had asked for a judicial commission subject to the repetition outside of Parliament of what was said in the House in 1985 by the hon member for Reservoir Hills. If he repeats that, our side will support a call for a judicial commission of inquiry.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Why do you not have a commission? Clear the air once and for all. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>I submit with all due respect&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, will the hon member Mr Nowbath take a question?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Certainly.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Will the hon member Mr Nowbath agree that wriggling does not become him? My question was his Ministers in 1987&#x2014;those who are Ministers now&#x2014;demanded a commission of inquiry and they made no conditions.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>They demanded a commission of inquiry when they were sitting on the other side of the House. Now, they have changed sides. They can speak for themselves.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Are you going to change their clothes?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Let us get this right, this rather remarkable exercise to which reference is now being made once again. It was not my intention to deal with this but there was this scheme to break up the National Peoples Party and to put Solidarity into power. I have been told&#x2014;I cannot vouch for the accuracy of what I have been told&#x2014;that the whole scheme was planned, initiated and executed by the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council in this House. He had his reasons for doing so. On this side there were two very ambitious people. One did hold a Ministerial position, but he was trying for the post of Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, and there was one crossover from the other side who had been engaged in hopping for a long time&#x2014;from this side to that side and back again. He, too, had great ambitions. He wanted to be Minister of Education. The hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, being the kind of politician he is, gathered his loyal supporters and arranged for them to take these two members to the other side, dump them there and come back! That has been done. They were left there and these people came back, and if they were rewarded it shows what clever politicians they are. You have to be clever in this game. You cannot be intelligent in this game.</p>

<p>Our side has undertaken to concede that a judicial commission of inquiry is necessary provided allegations are repeated outside this House. Unfortunately, however, my training as an academic politician, in the laws of Hindu logic and as a lawyer tell me that so far there is not a tittle of evidence to warrant a judicial commission of inquiry.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Can you use a word like that here?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>A tittle. Not the kind of tittle the hon member for Reservoir Hills is thinking of. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, by way of personal explanation I thought the hon member Mr Nowbath was using, he and I know quite well, tipple, not tittle.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Having tippled many, many short ones and bada pegs and chota pegs with the hon member for Reservoir Hills, I fully understand the meaning of tipple. In fact, I taught him to stop tippling and to start drinking properly. He will not deny that. He has already admitted it to several members of this House.</p>

<p>As far as a judicial commission of inquiry is concerned, I am not pursuing the matter as at this stage there is no evidence to warrant one.</p>

<p>One of these days we might well hear that just because the Chairman of the Minister&#x2019;s Council has blown a sphincter we need a judicial commission of inquiry. For any reason, at the drop of a hat, we get a call for a judicial commission of inquiry. I am beginning to wonder whether those who mouth the term loudest even know what a judicial commission of inquiry is, or whether they picked it up from somewhere and are repeating it time and again without even understanding it.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from><span class="col_223-224" refersTo="page_0134"/>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>[Inaudible.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>There is also the very vigorous attack by the hon member for Cavendish on the hon the Chairman of the Ministers&#x2019; Council, and his manhandling of Indian education. He has sent out a very pathetic cry in this House in past years: &#x201C;Please, let me have my White oppressors back.&#x201D; He wanted his White oppressors back. I am quoting him. [Interjection.] Of course, I grew up in a&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#devan">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P I DEVAN</person>:</from>

<p>May I make an observation so that it reaches Hansard. I said: &#x201C;This is what principals of schools have said.&#x201D;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>You referred to informers.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>He referred to informers. [Interjections.] Yes, I had more brains than to contest an election. [Interjections.] I am not a political&#x2026; [Interjections.] The hon member for Cavendish is totally frivolous, for the simple reason that in 1970, on the occasion of the 10th anniversary of the Republican festival, there was a solid call by the community: No participation in these celebrations.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>He participated, yes.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>However, what did he do?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#leader_of_the_official_opposition">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">LEADER OF THE OFFICIAL OPPOSITION</person>:</from>

<p>He participated.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>He created a committee for the purpose; he got an inspector to attend that, and departmental officials; he got his photograph in the paper. These days, however, as well as last year and the year before, he complains so much. He used those things, and he laid the ground for a subsequent promotion which, you recall, was held up for exactly one year. He received the promotion directly from a primary school to a high school&#x2014;one of those unequalled records in the history of Indian education. He went from a primary school, never having taught one day in a high school. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>Is that your level of debate? God help you, my friend. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>That was the pay-off he received for being the White man&#x2019;s &#x2018;good boy&#x2019;.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Have you been tippling?</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Not today, because the hon member for Reservoir Hills has not invited me. [Interjections.] I shall be willing to tipple this evening. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Having retired as school inspector, or what these days would be called an &#x2018;educator&#x2019;&#x2014;the terms change so fast and so often that one does not know whether one is a teacher, an educator or whatever it may be&#x2014;what happens? He joins the SAIC and supports the man who was then chairman of the&#x2026;</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>He is out of the party, and at that stage you were here. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>&#x2026; executive committee at the time. In addition, he appeared before the Chairman of the Executive Committee with a list of 22 teachers to be promoted.</p>

<p>He was turned down. That is a fact, Mr Chairman. He was turned down, and what did he do? He ran off like a scalded cat, to quote the hon member for Reservoir Hills in another document&#x2014;saying all kinds of nasty things about the hon the Minister and about the chairman of the executive committee. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>He next surfaced as a member of Solidarity. He contested an election on a Solidarity ticket and then, having no scruples about it, abandoned Solidarity when a carrot was dangled on this side of the House. [Interjections.] So he knows what carrots are.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Are you admitting that a bribe was offered by your side? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>I said that carrots were dangled.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>*An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Carrots!</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>*Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Do you understand Afrikaans? I am talking about carrots! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>You are doing well. Make those admissions! [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Any compliment from the hon the member for Stanger is not material to me. It has to come from people of political substance. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#moolla">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">Y MOOLLA</person>:</from>

<p>I do not talk through my posterior.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>That is the kind of language I would expect from a man like the hon member <span class="col_225-226" refersTo="page_0135"/>for Stanger. He can do no better. He has no thinking ability. He has no appreciation of what politics is, save that I do think he has given this House a lesson on the biology of cockroaches. [Interjections.] In fact, he has told me personally that he does not mind being called a cockroach. He says that as a student of biology, at least restricted to Std 8&#x2014;I do not know whether he passed Std 8 or Std 10&#x2014;he discovered that millions of years ago there were cockroaches. There were also dinosaurs and many other kinds of creatures. He said to me: &#x201C;Where are those dinosaurs? They are extinct, but those little cockroaches still survive.&#x201D; He said to me: &#x201C;So if you call me a cockroach, that is a compliment.&#x201D; I am not calling him a cockroach, Mr Chairman. That is what he told me. Therefore, as I was saying, when the hon member for Stanger proceeds to pay me what may be considered a left handed compliment, I understand fully that he can do no better. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>Since I have been talking about the political &#x201C;hoppings&#x201D; of the hon member for Cavendish, I should like at this stage to make it clear that he and I are very good friends outside this House. There is no question of that. I have known him since the days when he came from Cavendish in 1937 when I first met him. I do not know whether or not he has benefited from my knowledge. [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>He didn&#x2019;t; he is still on the other side of the House.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>No, he will come back again one of these days if the carrot is big enough. [Interjections.] He was a member of Solidarity. He made use of the Solidarity ticket. He called the now the hon the Minister of the Ministers&#x2019; Council all kinds of names when he left Solidarity. He came over to this side, and I should like to tell hon members about something which happened to me personally. At that time I was chairman of this House&#x2019;s Standing Select Committee on Education. Sure enough, the record shows that he became the chairman. I was pushed out. [Interjections.] That shows his &#x201C;push ability&#x201D;. He pushed me out of that position.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>Is that why you are against him? [Interjections.]</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>He then found that there appeared to be greener pastures to feed on on the other side.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Did you offer the Minister</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#hon_member">

<from>An <person refersTo="hansard_za">HON MEMBER</person>:</from>

<p>Did you offer the Minister</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>I did not, but my leader, I believe, did. I had no part in it. [Interjections.] When it comes to this kind of horse trading, I have no part in it. I only have to keep my ears open and receive messages, possibly by means of pigeons and cockroaches travelling from this side of the House to that side. They bring me the messages. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>During the course of his speech he also made certain allegations about promotions and transfers. However, instead of making those allegations, all he need do is what the leader of the PRP has done, namely to call the man in and get him to give him a sworn affidavit and then release it in this House or take it to the Police. However, there is not a single specific allegation. He talks about his &#x2018;contacts&#x2019; with the people in the Department of Education. So, in addition to his &#x2018;jumpability&#x2019; and his &#x2018;pushability&#x2019; he now has &#x2018;contactability&#x2019;. He has contacts in the Department of Education. However, I should be very happy to support his call for any kind of inquiry, provided he produces those affidavits and produces those men concerned who are making the allegations. I will take down the affidavits, Mr Chairman, and I will attest to them in his presence. All he has to do is to bring them to me. Then, when he has done that, I will say: &#x2018;Yes, now I ditch my leader, and I support a call for a judicial commission of inquiry&#x2019;. I am not prepared, however, to call for judicial commissions of inquiry just because someone has possibly blown a sphincter.</p>

<p>We have been sitting here this afternoon for almost four hours now, talking about ourselves&#x2014; about what one wants and what the allegations are&#x2014;totally ignoring the fact that outside this House there is a community that is embattled, that is beleaguered, and that is reaching the point of irrelevancy in the South African context. It is embattled.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#poovalingam">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">P T POOVALINGAM</person>:</from>

<p>It is also retching after seeing what is happening.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#nowbath">

<from>Mr <person refersTo="hansard_za">R S NOWBATH</person>:</from>

<p>Having taken a look at your face first thing in the morning, it induces that kind of feeling. [Interjections.]</p>

<p>All of us here&#x2014;on both sides of the House&#x2014;are engaged in a little local strife, but outside bigger things are happening. Moreover, and even outside, <span class="col_227-228" refersTo="page_0136"/>the extra-Parliamentary political forces are engaged in their own internecine strife. They hold themselves forth as leaders of the community but they have no constituencies. They themselves claim to be Gandhian in their outlook. They base their premises on Gandhian principles, yet what do they do? They hold their meetings in secret. Gandhi did not hold his meetings in secret. Nor did he plead &#x2018;not guilty&#x2019; in a court of law. He always made a public announcement to the effect that he would break a certain law at a particular time on a certain date and invited the Police to arrest him. When he appeared before a magistrate&#x2014;the first of these was the magistrate at Volksrust in the first passive resistance campaign in the country&#x2014;he said:</p>

<block name="quote">Impose upon me, Sir, the highest punishment which is available to this court.</block>

<p>Unfortunately for me, and I presume fortunately for hon members of the Opposition, my time has expired.</p>

</speech>

<speech by="#chairman_of_the_ministers_council">

<from>The <person refersTo="hansard_za">CHAIRMAN OF THE MINISTERS&#x2019; COUNCIL</person>:</from>

<p>Mr Chairman, I move:</p>

<block name="quote">That the debate do now adjourn.</block>

<p>Agreed to.</p>

</speech>

</debateSection>

<summary>(Motion)</summary>

<p>Mr Chairman, I move:</p>

<block name="quote">That the House do now adjourn.</block>

<p>Agreed to.</p>

</speech>

<p>The House adjourned at <recordedTime time="1988-02-08T18:02:00">18h02.</recordedTime></p>
